The seams at x = 260 − 4.2n are flush 0.5 mm #3B2B1F glue hairlines. Grommet: a Ø 2.0 cm hole through the back strip at 258/322 with a flush black ring #2A2C2E, for PROP-08. The work zone (y 250-340) is clean oiled oak, with no rings, stains, drip spots or knife marks, so no frame shows a mark the product made. - Mark 2 (texture only): front-edge wear at x 197-201, y 250-330, where her forearms rest while she brews. The tint is worn off the arris to satin #7E6045 (1.8:1), rounded to about a 6 mm radius. - Mark 3: a blue-black iron-water stain #25201C (1.6:1), 3 × 8 cm, at the sink's front corner, 204/185, from a wet cast-iron pan. Appears in no other location. | ST1 (D01-B1 until the D01-B4a set-down; D02-1): 232.7/280 is clean bare oiled #533C2B oak. PROP-03 stands at 232.7/295. ST2 (from the D01-B4a set-down, not before D01-B4a; D01-B5 to D01-B6; D02-2): PROP-01's foot stands on 232.7/280. From D01-B5, the mug's footprint at 232.7/295 is clean bare oak. The clear zone has no crumbs, dust, grounds or fingerprints, and the seams are closed hairlines. Mark 2 and Mark 3 are unchanged in every state. |
| PROP-08 | Charging cable and adapter: a 1 m USB-C cable with a straight plug and a black matte TPE jacket #2A2C2E, unbranded, plus a blank black cube adapter in OUT-01 under the top. It shows why the phone is waiting on this counter at 7:00: it charged here overnight, not by her bed. It reads 1.4:1 on the oak, below the product body's 7.3:1. Appears in no other location. | ST1 (D01-B1, until the flip at about 0.8 s): the plug is in the ST1 port at 222.3/304.8. The cable runs out along the phone's axis to x 218, then along x 218 toward wall C through a loose loop (x 210-218, y 308-318, about 10 cm of slack) to y 322. From there it runs along y 322 to x 258 and down through the grommet at 258/322 to the adapter in OUT-01, below the top. It never enters y < 302 and nothing runs up wall D, so it never touches the mug, the rings or the clear zone. ST2 (from the flip, not before D01-B1, through D01-B6): the plug turns 180° with the phone about its long axis, and the port moves 2 cm to 222.3/302.8. The loop gives up 2 cm and rolls half a turn, to x 211-218, y 308-316. The y 322 run and the grommet drop do not move. Locked in D01-B1, D01-B2, D01-B4a and D01-B4b: any length a frame sees holds this route exactly. In D01-B3 and D01-B5 to D01-B6 it is plugged in on this route, off frame. D02-1: unplugged and struck with the adapter. D02-2: struck. |


## Looks

### NORA_L01 (NORA)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: mid-price, bought once and kept. She spends on her work coat, boots and paper, and skimps on house clothes (retired knits, old sleepwear). At 7:00 she isn't dressed for anyone yet: she's in house clothes, with the work coat and boots still by the door (backstory only, nothing added to the set). The look says the day hasn't claimed her yet (Route R1). Story: a weekday at 7:00, early November 2026, USA (ASSUMED). It is about 6 °C outside and 19 °C in the kitchen while the radiator warms (ASSUMED). So she wears wool over a tee, trousers to the floor and socks: ankles covered, shoes by the door. Head to toe: (1) crewneck pullover, hem at the hip bone, sleeve ending in the lower third of the forearm, 7 cm above the wrist bone at rest and 9 cm when reaching or pouring; (2) short-sleeve crew tee under it, hem 3 cm below the pullover hem; (3) elastic-waist sleep trousers, full length, hem on the top of the foot; (4) crew socks to mid-calf; (5) no footwear. Older than the story: the pullover, bought around 2020. Two winters ago a 60 °C wash felted it and shrank the sleeves off her wrists. That made it her kitchen sweater, with nothing to push up before the pour. It is wardrobe's one real detail (taste notes: 'One real detail per scene') and the only garment the viewer sees (taste notes: 'Hands before faces'). Duplicates: use untreated, non-superwash 100% merino, because superwash won't felt. Start from a 12-gauge single-jersey crew one size up. Give it one 60 °C wash and one tumble dry, and a second dry only if it is still short of the numbers. Test one sample first, because shrink varies by yarn. Make 3, since a splash during the pour means a swap. Finished sizes, measured flat (ASSUMED from the 167 cm casting spec): sleeve 52 cm from shoulder seam to cuff edge; body 58 cm from neck-shoulder point to hem; chest 96 cm; cuff circumference 20 cm (forearm girth 7 cm above the wrist bone is about 19 cm, plus 1 cm ease). The fitting resets these on the hand double so the cuff lands 7 cm above the wrist bone with the hand flat on the counter. Every duplicate then matches the hero within ±0.5 cm. Pills: rub each inner forearm, 5-10 cm above the cuff, on the eased edge of an UNTINTED cured oiled-oak offcut until it matches the hero. Untinted, so the new tobacco tint on the PROP-07 finish can't transfer to the wool.
- **materials colors**: Pullover: untreated (non-superwash) 100% merino single jersey, 12-gauge, felted to about 330 g/m² (ASSUMED), ink blue #1F2A40. Matte and fuzzed flat, so no stitch reads. Cuff: a 4 cm band knitted 1x1 rib and felted solid in the same #1F2A40, with no wales visible. Small pills on both inner forearms, where she rests them on the counter's worn front edge (PROP-07 Mark 2). It holds its shape and doesn't slide. Risk: it sheds fibres, so lint-roll between setups; a blue fibre on the glaze or the oak means a reshoot. Tee (unseen): cotton single jersey 160 g/m², brown-black #2C2826. Trousers (unseen): brushed cotton flannel 170 g/m², slate grey #5A5E66. Socks (unseen): merino-nylon plain knit, charcoal #3A3A3D. Hair elastic (unseen): matte fabric-covered, black #2A2728 approx. Everything is plain unmarked fabric: no logo, label, stripe or print.
Approx LCh (sRGB, D65, hand-computed, all approx). Product (PROP-01): body #E3D9C6 L* 87, C* 11, h 88°; glaze pool #B59A72 L* 65, C* 25, h 80°; rim band #C9A77E L* 71, C* 27, h 76°; raw foot #A88663 L* 58, C* 25, h 71°. Product hues span 71-88°.
Pullover #1F2A40: L* 17, C* 16, h 279°, at least 152° from every product hue. Clears on hue.
Tee #2C2826: L* 16, C* 2, h 55°. It is 34° from the body (within 5° of the line), 25° from the pool, 21° from the rim and 16° from the foot. It clears on C* 2 and on L* (42+ below every surface). Nearest alternative that clears on hue: cool charcoal #2B2C30 (L* 18, C* 3, h 282°).
Trousers #5A5E66 (L* 40, C* 5, h 273°), socks #3A3A3D (L* 25, C* 2, h 291°) and elastic #2A2728 (L* 16, C* 2, h 354°) all clear on hue by 77° or more.
Built skin marks. These are casting anchors, not worn colour, and are never shifted to clear the product. Mole #3B2519 approx, right hand: L* 17, C* 15, h 55°. It clears on L*, 41+ below every product surface. Scar #8A6650 approx, left hand: L* 46, C* 21, h 58°. It is 13° from the foot, 18° from the rim, 22° from the pool and 30° from the body. As a worn colour it would fail against the foot, rim and pool (C* 21, only 12-25 L* apart). It stays because it is 10 × 1.5 mm, it sits on the hand that closes on the kettle handle in D02-1 rather than on the dripper, and styling never alters skin for separation.
Value: the sleeve (L* 17) is 18 below the skin #6F4A34 (L* 35, C* 23, h 56°) and 70 below the dripper body, which meets casting's value rule. It is 10 below the tinted oak #533C2B (L* 27, C* 16, h 62°) and 143° from it in hue, and 32 below the phone case #77756F (L* 49): blue against warm brown and neutral grey. The skin sits only about 8 L* above the new oak, so the sleeve gives the forearm its edge. The hand's own edge on the oak, and against the raw foot in the grip (23 L*), is the camera plan's job. Widest-frame read: warm brown hand plus ink-blue sleeve, the only blue on the set, inside the world bible's 8% out-of-palette share.
- **fit notes**: Fitted on the hand double's arms. Pullover: shoulder seam 1 cm inside the shoulder point (shrunk), 6 cm chest ease, hem at the hip bone. The sleeve is close through the forearm, and the cuff band has 1 cm ease, so it grips and can't slide down. The sleeve ends in the lower third of the forearm. At rest (hand flat, or heel of the hand on the oak as in D01-B4b) it sits 7 cm above the wrist bone. Reaching or pouring it sits 9 cm (±1) above: reaching means to the rack, the B2 lift or the B4a lift; pouring means the left arm raised with the kettle tipped, as in D02-1 (director's left-hand restage). It is never under 5 cm above the wrist bone on either arm (casting minimum), and both arms match. No fabric crosses the dripper, filter, kettle, stream, mug or phone in any reach. Tee: sleeve hem hidden at mid-bicep, body hem 3 cm below the pullover. Trousers: mid rise, 8 cm hip ease, full break, back hem 1 cm off the floor. Socks: tops at mid-calf, under the trousers. Closures: 0 on every garment; keep it at 0. In frame: only the cuffs and 5-15 cm of sleeve, in D01-B1, B2, B4a, B4b and D02-1. D01-B3 shows only the spout, so no wardrobe is in that frame.
- **accessories**: None on the hands or wrists: no ring, watch, bracelet, hair elastic, plaster, tape or finger cot. Per casting, a ring would click on stoneware over the designed sound, including the phone's tick against the mug at B4b, and a watch is a second clock against 7:00 and 7:05.; No apron and no towel over the shoulder, because either would drift into frame over the counter.; One matte black fabric-covered elastic, #2A2728 approx, in the hair only and never on the wrist.; No earrings, necklace or glasses: head and neck are never in frame.
- **hair makeup**: Skin, in casting's words. Back of hand and forearm: medium-deep, warm undertone, #6F4A34 approx. Palms and fingertip pads: medium, warm undertone, #B3886A approx. Knuckle creases: deep, warm undertone, #4A2F20 approx. Words and hexes go in the hand-double call and the acceptance checks. Prompts carry the words only (broad square hand, medium-deep skin, warm undertone, creased knuckles, short bare nails), because generators lighten, lengthen and smooth hands. Creases, veins and forearm hair are cast for, not built. The forearm hair #2A1E17 approx, sparse and fine along the outer edge of both forearms, stays as it is (the stylist's call, per casting), with no shaving on the day. No body makeup and no powder (it greys brown skin); pores stay visible. Finish matte: no hand cream or oil on the day; blot any sheen with a tissue. The only concealing: any mark of the double's own on either hand or forearm (mole, scar, tattoo) is covered with alcohol-activated paint matched to #6F4A34 and sealed matte, so the two built marks are the only marks.
Nails (joint lock with casting): no manicure, cuticle push-back or cuticle oil for 7 days before the shoot. On the day: free edge 1 mm or less, filed straight across, unpolished and unbuffed. The right thumb cuticle is left untrimmed so it reads slightly ragged.
MOLE, right hand only. Flat, round, 4 mm (±0.3), #3B2519 approx, matte, with a 0.3 mm soft edge and no relief. With the hand flat, it sits on the back of the right hand, on the line of the middle-finger bone, centred 2 cm toward the wrist from the middle-finger knuckle. At the fitting, cut a clear acetate template to the double's right hand, mark the knuckles and wrist bone on it, and punch a 4 mm hole at that point. Primary method: a printed skin-safe transfer, 10 printed from one file, with the print proofed against a #3B2519 swatch card in daylight. Backup: alcohol-activated paint stippled through the hole. Clean the skin with an alcohol wipe, let it dry, apply, then seal matte.
SCAR, left hand only (new from casting). A pale, flat, matte line, 10 mm (±1) long and 1.5 mm wide, with tapered ends and no relief, #8A6650 approx (lighter than the skin around it). It runs across the back of the left index finger, side to side, centred on the crest of the middle joint with the finger straight. Method: alcohol-activated paint mixed to a #8A6650 swatch card (a transfer would crack over a flexing joint). Apply it through a 10 × 1.5 mm slot in a left-hand acetate template marked at the index knuckle and middle joint, then seal with a flexible matte sealer. The joint's creases still read through as texture; only the tone lifts. Then close the hand fully on a dowel the size of the kettle handle 5 times. If the scar cracks, smears or shines, redo it before camera.
Patch-test every product on the double 48 h before the shoot. Photograph the fitting application on both hands beside a ruler; that photo is the reference.
CHECK AND REAPPLY at the start of each hand setup (D01-B1, B2, B4a, B4b, D02-1), and again after any hand wash or contact with water, coffee or steam:
(1) Right hand flat, template on. The mole sits in the hole within ±1 mm, measures 4 mm, matches its card, is matte and has an unbroken edge.
(2) Left hand flat, template on. The scar sits in the slot within ±1 mm, matches its card, and has no crack or shine.
(3) No other mark on either hand or forearm.
(4) Photograph both hands beside the ruler and log the time and the mole unit number.
Fixes: shine gets re-sealed. Anything else (a smudge, drift over 1 mm, a crack, a lift, colour off the card) means remove it, reapply and re-run the check. On the first take of each setup, confirm on the monitor that the mole is on the right hand and the scar is on the left.
Generation: every generated frame showing the back of the right hand is checked against CHR-NORA-HAND@v1 for the mole. Every generated frame showing the back of the left hand is checked against CHR-NORA-HAND@v1 for the scar. No hex codes go in prompts: identity lives in the reference and the acceptance check.
Hair (ASSUMED, never in frame): black-brown #2A1E17 approx to match the forearm hair; wavy (2B), to mid shoulder blade, centre part. Twist it into a low knot at the nape with the one elastic, so no strand falls forward over the pour. Face: not cast, no makeup, never in frame (taste notes: 'Hands before faces'). She reads by two colour blocks (brown hand, ink-blue sleeve) plus casting's broad, square hand shape.
- **continuity locks**: [every frame with her: D01-B1, B2, B4a, B4b, D02-1] The sleeve ends in the lower third of the forearm: 7 cm above the wrist bone at rest, 9 cm (±1) reaching or pouring, never under 5 cm. The wrist and hand below it are bare. Check in the hand insert.; [every frame with her] The only visible sleeve is the felted ink-blue house pullover. No shirt cuff, blazer sleeve or work fabric shows under or over it.; [every frame with her] Hands and wrists carry nothing: no ring, watch, bracelet, hair elastic, ring mark, plaster or tape.; [widest counter frame, D01-B4a] The only fabric in frame is one flat ink-blue #1F2A40 solid, cuff included, with no stripe, cable, print, label or second colour.; [insert] The cuff is a 4 cm band in the same #1F2A40, felted solid with no wales visible, with fuzz and small pills on the inner forearm. A crisp or visibly ribbed cuff is wrong.; [any frame showing the back of the right hand] One flat, matte, 4 mm dark-brown mole #3B2519 with an unbroken edge, centred 2 cm toward the wrist from the middle-finger knuckle. The left hand has no mole. A mole on the left hand means the frame is mirrored: reject.; [any frame showing the back of the left hand, at least D02-1 on the kettle handle] One pale, flat, matte 10 mm scar #8A6650 across the back of the index finger's middle joint. The right hand has no scar. A scar on the right hand means the frame is mirrored: reject.; [macro] Nails short, bare and straight-filed; right thumb cuticle untrimmed; backs of both hands matte with no sheen; sparse dark forearm hair along the outer edge of both forearms, unshaved.; [every frame] No hair, head or collar in frame, and no strand or wool fibre on the dripper, the filter, the pour or the oak.

## Director's treatment

### S1

- **performance beats**: NORA's scene. Hero beat: B1 (SH01), close on the flip. She moves at habit pace from her first frame (casting). The only urgency belongs to the phone, and it ends at the flip.

B1 (0.0-1.5, SH01). Objective: get the coffee going before the day gets anything. Obstacle: PROP-02 is lit at 7:00 and buzzing beside her mug. Action: R lands on it without slowing, turns it face-down so it settles against the mug, and carries on toward the rack in one move, with no pause and no glance. Real detail: she flips it while it is still plugged in. It charged here overnight (PROP-08), and she doesn't unplug it to refuse it. The tide line inside the empty mug (the world bible's S1 detail) reads in the same frame.

B2 (1.5-4.5, SH02A + SH02B). Objective: get the dripper on the mug before the kettle is ready. Obstacle: the dripper is drying in the rack, 82 cm from the mug. Action: R takes PROP-01 the way she'd take an empty cup. Index and middle fingers hook over the near rim into the dry cone, with the thumb outside. As the grip closes, the thumb lands at the foot and draws up the front across the glaze pool without stopping (script). She carries it low and level, seats it on the mug with a click, lets go and leaves it there, with the hand clear of the phone. Filter and grounds go in during the B2/B3 cut (script).

Hands: R does every action in S1. L hangs at her side through the rack trip, which falls in the cuts. From SH02B's first frame, L lies flat on the front edge at y≈320, off frame.
Entry: NORA at N2, both hands at her sides. PROP-02 ST1, PROP-08 ST1, PROP-03 ST1 (empty, tide line visible), PROP-04 ST1, PROP-01 ST1 (dry, in the rack, front at 235°). PROP-05 is in the upper cabinet. PROP-06 is heating off-set.
Exit: PROP-01 and PROP-03 at ST2 (empty dripper seated). PROP-04 ST2. PROP-02 and PROP-08 at ST2 (face-down, touching the mug, still plugged in). R has gone toward wall A. L lies flat at y≈320.
- **blocking**: Only her hand and forearm are seen. The prop marks are the world bible's. NORA's marks are mine, worked out from reach.

N2 (start and return): centred at y 308, hips 2 cm off the front edge (x≈195), facing 90°. Chest at y 289-327; right shoulder at about 185/293, 137 cm up (ASSUMED).
Rack mark: centred at y 215 (chest at y 196-234, right shoulder at about 185/196). From here the dripper's near side is at about 250°, inside the front's thick arc (about 201-269°), so the rim hook puts her thumb on the front without turning the dripper. The trip N2 → rack mark → N2 falls inside the two elliptical cuts, so no frame shows her stepping.

Line of action along the counter:
- Out, toward wall A: R leaves the phone, passes the mug and goes to the rack.
- Back, the opposite way: R carries the dripper toward wall C along the x 232.7 strip, foot 12 ±2 cm over the oak, to the mug (232.7/295).
- After the seat: R withdraws back along the carry line toward wall A, where the filters are kept, and never passes over the phone.

Frames. Every lens is in the aisle on the wall-A side, looking past her right flank, so her torso is never in frame.
- SH01 (55°): about 34 cm across at the phone, rack out (ruling 1). It holds PROP-02, the mug with its tide line, and PROP-08 wherever it shows (lock 02/03/08). The sightline passes x 195 at y 280, 9 cm off her flank. R exits frame right, toward the rack.
- SH02A (25°, on the rack): about 40 cm across, with a fallback of about 25 cm (see flags). The front is 30° off the lens, so the thumb crosses the pool on the lens side, left of centre. R enters from frame left. On plan, her nearest torso corner is about 27 cm left of the line to the dripper and 31 cm nearer the lens, which is outside the frame (Cinematographer to confirm, blocker 1).
- SH02B (55°), with NORA back at N2: it holds PROP-01, PROP-03, PROP-02 touching the mug, and PROP-08 wherever it shows. The dripper travels right to left.

Card 'Nobody gets you before the pour.': in at 1.5 s on the SH01/SH02A cut and out at 4.5 s, across both angles (script). World position: the card area the world bible keeps bare on the wall-D paint (125 cm up and above, y 240-340), in the upper third of both frames. In SH02A it sits above the rim, clear of the thumb's whole path from foot to rim band. In SH02B it sits above the carry, where the dripper and hand stay below about 115 cm. D01 is native 9:16, so nothing is cropped.
- **action progression**: SH01, B1 (0.0-1.5):
- 0.0-0.8: PROP-02 lies face-up with '7:00' and the banner lit, buzzing in its footprint. The eye is on '7:00'.
- 0.8-1.2: R lands without slowing (ruling 9): thumb on the wall-C long edge, fingertips on the mug-side edge. It lifts the phone 2 cm, turns it over its long axis toward the mug and lets go 1 cm above the oak. The phone lands face-down 2 cm nearer the mug, with its mug-side edge against the mug. Knock; the buzz ends (PROP-02 and PROP-08 to ST2).
- 1.2-1.5: R lifts off toward wall A and exits frame right past the mug.
Elliptical cut at 1.5 s, and the card comes in.

SH02A, B2 first angle (1.50-2.25, 18 f):
- f1-6 (1.50-1.75): R arrives over the rack's aisle-side wires. Index and middle fingers hook the near rim. The thumb lands at the foot on the front and, as the grip closes, sweeps up across the pool to the rim band without stopping.
- f7-14 (1.75-2.08): R lifts the dripper about 14 cm straight up until the foot clears the 13 cm wires. Faint knock off the tray (PROP-01 and PROP-04 to ST2).
- f15-18 (2.08-2.25): R sets off toward wall C, level, for about 5 cm.
Elliptical cut mid-carry at 2.25 s (script). About 65 cm of the carry falls in the cut.

SH02B, B2 second angle (2.25-4.50, 54 f):
- f1-10: opens with the dripper in the right third, 12 cm short of the mug with its foot 12 cm over the oak, already moving right to left. It carries 12 cm.
- f11-15: R lowers it the last 2.5 cm onto the rim. Click on f15.
- f16-20: the fingers unhook, the thumb comes off, and R rises 3 cm.
- f21-30: R withdraws along the carry line toward wall A and exits frame right.
- f31-54: the empty dripper sits on the mug, and the mug still touches the phone. Nothing moves.

1.5 + 0.75 + 2.25 = 4.5 s, and every shot is under 5 s. SH02A's 18 f stand (ruling 6), because SH02B's f15 seat is the product's first full look. Peak hand speed is about 0.6 m/s or below.
- **emotional shift**: NORA holds. Her hand moves at one speed onto the phone and off it, because a visible decision would be acting (casting). The shift belongs to the frame.
- Before: the phone is face-up, lit and buzzing. The day asks first.
- Trigger: her hand lands on it at 0.8 s.
- After: by 1.2 s the screen is down, dark and silent, and the same hand is already moving at the same speed toward the dripper.
The dripper plays no part in the trigger, so nothing credits it with calming her. The flip leaves the phone touching her mug, which B4b pays off.
- **transition in**: Cold open, hard in on frame 1. The phone is already lit and the buzz starts on frame 1, with no fade. The eye sits on '7:00' until R arrives at 0.8 s.
- **transition out**: 1.5 s, SH01 to SH02A: an elliptical cut on direction (ruling 2). R exits SH01 frame right and enters SH02A from frame left onto the dripper, so screen travel stays left to right and the trip to the rack falls in the cut. The eye goes from the hand leaving past the mug (right third) to the hand landing on the rim (left of centre). The card comes in on this cut. Silence crosses it, because the knock ended at 1.2 s.

2.25 s, SH02A to SH02B: an elliptical cut mid-carry (script). The dripper moves right to left on both axes at the same height over the oak. The eye stays on the dripper as it goes from centre to the right third. The card holds, and room tone crosses.

4.5 s, out to S2: a same-axis cut-in on 55°, across the ellipsis in which the filter and grounds go in. PROP-01 on PROP-03 carries across. The eye goes from the empty dripper on the mug to the heaped grounds at SH03's centre. The card ends on the cut, and room tone crosses it.
- **campaign idea link**: 'Before the phone, the inbox, or anyone else gets a piece of you, there are five minutes that are only yours — marked by the pour.' Carried by the B1 flip of PROP-02 (SH01) and by the same R taking PROP-01 in B2, with the thumb crossing its glaze pool (SH02A). Cut this and the viewer loses the choice itself: the phone refused at 7:00 and the dripper taken instead. They also lose the phone left against her mug, which B4b needs, and the first touch of the mark that B5 names as 'Hand-glazed stoneware.'
- **practical or compositing flags**: SH01 legible screen text. Method: composite on the practical plate. The real phone shows a flat #E4E7EA field, so its light on the oak and her hand is real. Acceptance: in the 34 cm rack-out frame, with the digits about 35° off upright, '7:00' reads on a 6-inch phone at 100% from 0.0 to 0.8 s. The banner's two lines stay unreadable. There is no other glyph, status bar or logo, and the digits stay locked to the glass until the phone goes edge-on at about 1.0 s. Risk: an unreadable '7:00' costs the hook and the 7:05 payoff.; SH01 flip, the hero beat. It combines finger work, a turn in the air, a live cable and contact with the mug. Method: practical plate with a hand double matched to NORA's anchors (release pending, blocker 7). Acceptance: the grip is as ruled, turned in one continuous 0.4 s move. The phone lands face-down at 230.0/302.8, touching the mug, and the mug stays on 232.7/295. The cable stays in the port and its loop rolls to ST2. The R mole reads, and the hand's speed is the same before and after. The knock is the real case on the real oak. Risk: a gap between the phone and the mug leaves B4b's rings no path. Retake it; don't fix it in post.; SH02A thumb pass and lift. This is a hand on the product doing fine finger work, and it is the first sight of the glaze pool that later carries 'hand-glazed' (claim_demo_needs_clearance, with SH06 and SH08). Method: practical on the real unit with the hand double. The fallback is a pickup, not generation (ruling 10). At the prelight the PD confirms that the rack's aisle-side wires leave the thumb a clear path to the foot (about 8 cm on plan). Acceptance: on a 6-inch phone at 100%, with the card on, the thumb visibly lands on the foot and sweeps up over the pool without stopping, on the lens side of the front's thick arc. Glaze shows on both sides of the hand. The front stays at 235° and is never turned. The foot clears the wires with one faint knock. If the sweep doesn't read at about 40 cm, SH02A tightens to about 25 cm, which also keeps her torso further out. If the card still pulls the eye off the thumb, see blocker 5. Risk: a thumb that misses the arc, or doesn't read, loses B5's setup, and one that stops reads as a caress for the camera.; SH02B carry and seat, with hands on the product. Method: practical on the real unit. Acceptance: the dripper travels level at 12 ±2 cm over the oak and never drops below the mug's 9.5 cm rim height until it is over the mug. It is lowered 2.5 cm onto the rim in one move, with the click on f15 and the raw band hanging 2 mm outside the rim. The front stays at 235°. The hand leaves toward wall A and never passes over the phone, and the phone still touches the mug afterwards. The click is the real unit on the real mug, not sweetened. Risk: a dip-and-pop carry reads as fake, and a nudged phone breaks B4b.; Bodies in SH01 to SH02B. Method: practical, with the double's feet on the taped N2 and rack marks and every take checked on the monitor. Acceptance: her torso, upper arm and L never enter SH01, SH02A (at either frame size) or SH02B. Risk: a hip or shoulder at the frame edge breaks the hands-only casting.

### S2

- **performance beats**: NORA's scene, played by her left hand off frame. Hero beat: B3 (SH03), close on the stream meeting the grounds.

B3 (4.5-7.0). Objective: start the bloom from the centre out without the stream touching the paper. Obstacle: water that hits the paper runs down past the coffee. Action: L brings PROP-06 in by its beech loop handle, with the forearm still and only the wrist circling (casting). The stream makes two circuits, from the centre out to a finger's width short of the paper and back. Then she stops it clean and the spout lifts out. Only the spout is in frame (script).

Deliberate break of the taste note 'One real detail per scene': D01-B3 carries the pour alone, and S2's detail, the kettle's scorch band, moves to D02-1 (SH07). The reason: SH03's frame is about 20 cm wide, and the kettle body sits 14-27 cm frame-left of the cone, so holding the band would halve the bloom. The script makes the same choice.

Hands (restaged): L pours. The handle is on her left, so L takes it from her left shoulder with a natural grip and no cross. From this kettle position R would have to pour cross-body with a backhand grip. R hangs at her side, off frame, in B3 and D02-1. Nothing touches the product while it performs.
Entry, after the ellipsis: L holds PROP-06 just off frame-left, with the spout not yet in, and R is at her side. PROP-05 ST3 (dry heap, peak 3.0 cm below the rim). PROP-01 ST3 on PROP-03. PROP-02 ST2. PROP-08 ST2, plugged in, off frame.
Exit: L and the kettle are out frame-left. PROP-05 ST4 (bloomed). PROP-03 ST3 (coffee rising, light steam).
Everything plays at 100% speed (taste reject: slow motion used to make something look expensive).
- **blocking**: NORA stands at N2, facing 90°. Her left shoulder is at about 185/323.

Kettle at the pour: the body is centred at about 220/311, level with the cone in depth. The spout runs toward about 140°, square to the lens, so it reads in profile. The handle is at about 215/317, 9 cm to her left of midline. L reaches it about 31 cm forward and slightly right of her left shoulder, with no cross and no lean. The spout tip sits 1-3 cm over the rim at the cone centre (232.7/295). The kettle comes in along the spout's line from about 7 cm back and leaves the same way, then goes off frame toward the aisle and the burner (212/97). The stream stays inside the cone.

SH03 (55°, about 20 cm across): the sightline to the cone passes x 195 at y≈269, 20 cm clear of her. In frame: the spout entering from frame left, the stream, the cone, the mug, and the phone's dark face-down wall-D end at lower left (ruling 3; continuity with SH02B and SH04). Out of frame-left: the kettle body, the scorch band, L, R, the phone's port end and PROP-08 (lock: PROP-02's wall-D end, 03, 05, 06). The 'bottom edge above the counter' requirement and its paint-out fallback are withdrawn.

D02-1 (SH07): same marks at the same moment, on a separate 4:5 plate staged wider. It is not a crop of SH03. L's fingers on the beech handle show at the top-left corner, cropped to include the index finger's middle joint so the scar reads. This is the one exception to 'L never in frame'. In frame: the kettle body with its scorch band, the stream landing on the bloom, the whole dripper, the mug, and PROP-02 face-down, dark and unplugged against the mug. PROP-08 is struck, and 232.7/280 is clean oak.
- **action progression**: SH03, B3 (4.5-7.0), one uncut take of 60 f:
- 4.5-4.7: the heaped grounds sit alone at frame centre, still.
- 4.7-4.9: the spout comes in from frame left along its own line and stops over the peak.
- 4.9-6.5: L tips the kettle, and a thin stream lands on the peak. It makes two circuits, from the centre out to a finger's width short of the paper and back. The grounds darken outward from the centre and start to rise (PROP-05 ST3 to ST4).
- 6.5-6.7: L tips back, the stream stops clean, and the spout lifts out frame-left.
- 6.7-7.0: the bloom holds alone, still fizzing.

One pass mark for every take: by 6.7 s the whole visible bed has darkened, and the centre has risen enough to see on a 6-inch phone at 100%. The minimum is about 5 mm, and about 1 cm is the target, not the pass. The grounds bloom on screen for 2.1 s, inside the route's 2-3 s hold. The take starts and ends on a holdable state (the heap alone; the bloom alone). SH07 captures the moment at about 5.7 s: the second outward circuit landing on the bloom.
- **emotional shift**: Hold. No trigger arrives, and none should: her wrist keeps one pace from the first drop to the last. The only change is physical, from a dry heap to a bloom. A visible reaction to the bloom would claim that the dripper relaxes her, which is an unverified benefit.
- **transition in**: A same-axis cut-in on 55° from SH02B, across the ellipsis in which the filter and grounds go in. The grounds are already heaped in a pressed filter (script). The eye goes from the empty dripper on the mug to the heap at frame centre, and the spout arrives there 0.2 s later. Room tone crosses the cut, and the first new sound is the stream at 4.9 s.
- **transition out**: A hard cut at 7.0 s on the same 55° axis, widening to SH04 across the 'MOMENTS LATER' ellipsis (the drawdown, and the kettle going back to its burner). PROP-01 on PROP-03 carries across. The eye goes from the bloom at frame centre to the dripper on the mug, with R already in its grip. The fizz stops dead on the cut, and room tone marks the time passing. The first new sound is the last drip, at about 7.25 s.
- **campaign idea link**: 'Before the phone, the inbox, or anyone else gets a piece of you, there are five minutes that are only yours — marked by the pour.' Carried by B3 (SH03): the PROP-06 stream spiralling into PROP-01 and the grounds blooming inside it. Cut this and the viewer loses the pour that marks the five minutes, and the dripper never works on screen. The spot would jump from refusing the phone to a finished cup, with no tool before the hero (taste note).
- **practical or compositing flags**: SH03 stream and bloom, which are the product's own output. Method: practical only, on the real unit with real water and coffee at 100% speed. There is no generated fallback. Acceptance: one thin, continuous stream from the 5 mm spout that lands only on the grounds, never on the paper. The take meets the single pass mark (whole visible bed darkened, centre visibly risen by at least about 5 mm by 6.7 s, on a 6-inch phone at 100%). The paper darkens no higher than 1 cm below the rim, the collar stays dry, and nothing overflows. No speed ramp, and no foam, bubbles or steam added in post. A take below the pass mark is not used. If no take passes, blocker 3's fix applies. Risk: a ramped or over-foamed bloom overstates what the dripper does.; SH03 and SH07 left-hand pour (restaged). Method: practical, with the hand double pouring left-handed. It is rehearsed at the prelight, after casting's left-hand test. Acceptance: the forearm stays still and only the wrist circles, and the stream never wobbles or gets corrected. R never enters either frame. In SH07 the L scar reads at the index finger's middle joint, and no mole shows. Risk: an unsteady pour with her weaker hand reads as unpractised and breaks casting's years-of-habit read. The wrong anchor on the pouring hand breaks continuity.; SH03 frame contents. Method: framing (the Cinematographer's). The PD supplies PROP-06 and PROP-03 unmarked. Acceptance: the phone's wall-D end reads dark at lower left. No port, cable, kettle body, scorch band or hand is in frame, and no text or mark shows on the spout or the mug. Risk: a stray cable, glow or invented brand pulls the eye off the bloom.; SH07 (D02-1) still: liquids, with a hand on the kettle. Method: practical, with the stream frozen by the exposure. The hand-double release is pending (blocker 7). Acceptance: L's fingers on the beech handle at the top-left corner. The 1 cm scorch band (#6E3A2A) reads at the kettle base. The stream lands on the bloom, and the dripper is whole and unobstructed. The phone lies face-down, dark and unplugged against the mug, and PROP-08 is struck. Copy space stays clear of the dripper, the kettle and the pour. Risk: a split stream, a splash on the outer glaze, or a scorch band cleaned off, which would lose S2's detail.

### S3

- **performance beats**: NORA's scene. Hero beat: B4b (SH05), with her hand, the coffee surface and the phone in one frame.

B4a (7.0-8.5, SH04). Objective: get the dripper off the mug without dripping on the oak. Obstacle: a last drop is hanging at the exit hole. Action: R lifts PROP-01 3 cm and holds it over the coffee until the drop falls, and the drop ends the hold. Then, in one arc, R carries it 15 cm toward wall A and sets it on the oak just past the mug, away from the phone, which uncovers the coffee. Grip: a claw over the rim, with the fingertips on the outer glaze under the rim band and the palm clear of the spent filter's collar. Real detail: she waits out the last drip over the mug instead of carrying a dripping cone.

B4b (8.5-11.5, SH05). Objective: take her coffee as soon as it is cool enough to hold. Obstacle: it is too hot to grip, and the phone rattles against it. Action: the heel of R rests on the oak in front of the mug. The fingertips run along the mug's aisle side below the rim, with the palm clear and the thumb along the index, so the hand touches the mug without gripping it. When the phone rattles and rings run through the coffee, nothing in the hand moves: no flinch, no squeeze and no drift toward the phone. The rattle stops, the rings flatten, and her hand stays where it is (script). Real detail: fingertips on a just-brewed mug with the palm held off it, which is how she waits every morning. Scene detail: the rings bending TILE-01's reflected edge (script, world bible).

Entry, after the ellipsis: R in its claw on PROP-01, still. PROP-01 on PROP-03, with PROP-05 ST5 in the cone and a drop hanging (blocker 4). PROP-03 ST4. PROP-02 ST2, touching the mug. PROP-08 ST2. PROP-06 is on its burner, off frame. L lies flat on the front edge at y≈320, off frame.
Exit: R still on the mug. PROP-01 ST4 on 232.7/280, with the spent filter in. PROP-02 dark and back against the mug. The coffee is flat.
- **blocking**: NORA stands at N2: centred at y 308, hips 2 cm off the edge, facing 90°, right shoulder at about 185/293. Only R crosses the front edge, and only between y≈280 and y≈300. L rests flat at y 315-325.

B4a: R lifts PROP-01 3 cm straight up off PROP-03 (232.7/295). It carries the dripper 15 cm toward wall A along the x 232.7 strip, with the foot clearing the rim before it descends, and sets it on clean oak at 232.7/280 (±1 cm), front at 235°. The phone, the mug and the dripper now sit in a line.
B4b: R comes about 15 cm back toward wall C, opposite the way it went out, and settles. The heel sits on the oak at about 225.7/295, 3 cm in front of the mug and at least 2 cm clear of the case (y ≥299). The pads rest over 225-315° below the rim, with the thumb inside 315°. The handle (90°), the chip (195°) and the phone contact (0°) stay clear. The arm is nearly straight, with light weight on the heel and no lean. Her wrist crossing the phone's port end in frame is accepted (ruling 5).

Frames:
- SH04 and SH05, the plan of record as one take: 55°, from the aisle past her right flank, about 42 cm across at the dripper's plane, 1.7x SH06. The sightlines pass x 195 at y≈254 (to 280) and y≈269 (to the mug). The frame holds the dripper at 280, the coffee surface (the view must see into the mug once B4a uncovers it), all of PROP-02, PROP-05 in the cone, and PROP-08 wherever it shows (lock 02/03/05/08). The rack's wall-C corner (220/236) is about 29 cm frame-right of the line to 280, so it stays out, and so does WIN-01.
- SH05 reframed, only if blocker 2 fails at 42 cm: about 30 cm across, on a bearing between 31° and 55° so TILE-01 stays in the coffee's mirror path (world bible). The dripper, mug and phone stack up the 9:16 frame instead of running across it, and the dripper stays in frame for the whole beat (script). Her torso stays out: at 40°, the sightline to the mug passes x 195 at y≈250. SH04 then cuts to SH05 at the settle (ruling 4 reopened).

The card 'Everyone else gets you at 7:05.' sits in the world bible's bare card area (wall-D paint, 125 cm up and above, y 240-340), clear of the dripper, the coffee and the hand.
- **action progression**: SH04-SH05 is one locked practical take from 7.0 to 11.5 s: 108 f, under 5 s (ruling 4). The drop, the settle and the rings are live events, so the beat runs on cues, not the clock, and the still-hand tail absorbs the drift.

SH04, B4a (nominal 7.00-8.50, 36 f):
- 7.00-7.17 (4 f): R lifts PROP-01 3 cm off the rim and holds it level.
- 7.17-7.33 (4 f nominal): the hanging drop falls into the coffee: plink. The drop ends the hold. Accepted window: 7.17-7.42.
- 7.33-7.75 (10 f): 2 f after the drop, R carries the dripper in one arc 15 cm toward wall A, clear of the rim, down onto 232.7/280: stoneware on wood (PROP-01 ST4, PROP-07 ST2).
- 7.75-8.00 (6 f): release. The fingertips open off the outer glaze, and R rises 2 cm clear of the collar.
- 8.00-8.50 (12 f): R travels about 15 cm back. The heel is down by 8.30 and the pads are on the glaze by 8.50. Settled.

SH05, B4b (nominal 8.50-11.50):
- Settle to +5 f: the hand is still.
- 5 f after the settle (nominal 8.71), props fire the buzz, about 0.8 s long. PROP-02 lights face-down (edge glow, a secondary cue), rattles 3 mm at most and ticks against the mug, and fine rings run across the coffee (PROP-02 ST3).
- Nominal 9.50: the rattle stops, the phone settles back against the mug, the glow goes, and the rings flatten.
- The 7:05 card comes up as the rings flatten, within 9.25-9.75 s (script: 9.5 s).
- To 11.50: the hand holds and nothing moves. This tail absorbs any drift, so the cut stays at 11.5 s.

A drop anywhere in its window puts the card between 9.42 and 9.67 s. Take-selection priority: drop, landing, rings, still hand. The take starts on a holdable state (the hand still in its claw) and ends on one (the hand still on the mug, the coffee flat). Join fallback: at the settle (nominal 8.5 s), with 5 still frames after the join and none before. If blocker 2 forces the reframe, this join becomes the cut into the 30 cm SH05.
- **emotional shift**: - Before (7.0-8.5): the hand hasn't stopped since 0.8 s. Every object it reaches, it takes, moves and leaves.
- Trigger (nominal 8.71 s): the phone rattles against the mug she is touching. The tick is audible, and rings run through the coffee under her fingers.
- After: the hand doesn't move, not toward the phone and not tighter. It is still on the mug at 11.5 s, after the phone has gone quiet and the coffee has flattened.
Visible change: for the first time in the spot, her hand stays on something instead of moving on, and it outlasts the phone. The dripper stands on the oak, out of the exchange, so no calm is credited to it.
- **transition in**: A hard cut at 7.0 s from SH03 on the same 55° axis, widening across the ellipsis. PROP-01 is still on PROP-03, now spent, with R already in its claw. The eye goes from the bloom to the dripper on the mug. Silence crosses the cut, and the plink (nominal 7.25 s) is the first new sound.
- **transition out**: At 11.5 s: a same-axis 1.7x cut-in on the time jump into SH06 at C5. The hand and mug vanishing carry the cut, and the size change marks it. Across the jump, the hand, the mug, its steam and the spent filter are gone. PROP-01 (reset exactly to 232.7/280) and the face-down PROP-02 keep their world positions. The eye goes from the still hand and the flat coffee to the dripper, right of centre beside the bare oak where the mug stood. The 7:05 card ends on the cut, and 'Hand-glazed stoneware.' comes on with the 'Solace' sign-off. Room tone crosses the cut, and the kettle's cooling tick starts after it. If SH05 was reframed, this becomes a hard cut on the time jump with the same carries: the dripper sits lower-right in SH05 and lands just right of centre in SH06.
- **campaign idea link**: 'Before the phone, the inbox, or anyone else gets a piece of you, there are five minutes that are only yours — marked by the pour.' Carried by B4b (SH05): PROP-02's second buzz reaches her coffee through the mug it was flipped against, shows as rings under R, and gets nothing back. The 7:05 card names when it will get her. Cut this and the viewer loses the proof that the five minutes hold past the first refusal, and when they end. The flip becomes a one-off gesture.
- **practical or compositing flags**: SH04 last drip, which is the product's own output. Method: practical at 100% speed, relying on the drip timing (blocker 4). Acceptance: one drop leaves the exit hole into the coffee between 7.17 and 7.42 s. No drop lands on the outer glaze, the mug rim or the oak during the carry. Any later drop after the set-down falls inside the foot ring, out of sight, and props wipe it at the SH06 reset. Risk: a drip on the outside reads as a leaky dripper, and a missing drop loses the real detail.; SH04 set-down and release. Method: practical takes, each checked against the taped mark on the monitor. Acceptance: the foot lands within ±1 cm of 232.7/280, by hand and without looking, with the front at 235° and the foot clearing the rim before it descends. The release takes at least 5 f (6 planned) and doesn't nudge the dripper. The fingertips never touch the spent filter, and the oak shows no drip spot or mark. The take holds wherever the dripper lands, and props reset it exactly for SH06. Risk: a spot on the oak reads as a mark the product leaves.; SH05 coffee rings, which carry the turn with the sound off. Method: practical, only after blocker 2 passes at SH05's frame size. No composited rings. Acceptance, judged on the finished shot with the card laid in, on a 6-inch phone at 100%: TILE-01's reflected edge is seen to bend in the coffee during the rattle, and it is straight again as the card comes up. The rings start with the rattle, never before, and the card never covers the coffee. Her knuckles and fingertips don't move frame to frame, and the mug doesn't slide. If the bend doesn't read at 42 cm, SH05 is reframed at about 30 cm, with the settle join as plan of record (blocker 2). Risk: rings too small to read lose the sound-off turn, and rings that seem to come from her hand read as a tremor, turning the refusal into nerves.; SH05 hand still. Method: practical, with no generated plate. Acceptance: the heel stays on the oak at about 225.7/295. The pads stay over 225-315° below the rim, with the palm clear and the thumb along the index. The handle, the chip and the phone contact stay clear, and the hand stays at least 1 cm off the case in plan. No finger moves from the settle to 11.5 s. Risk: a fidget reads as anxiety and softens the refusal.; SH05 phone glow and rattle, a secondary cue. Method: the rattle is practical, from the real buzz. The glow is practical, and any composite matches a reference plate of the real phone face-down in its case under the SH05 light. Acceptance: the glow matches the reference in which edges leak, how far the light reaches (5-15 mm, ASSUMED) and how bright it is. Nothing is legible. The glow lasts only as long as the buzz. The rattle travels 3 mm at most, and the phone ends touching the mug. Risk: a glow brighter or wider than the reference tips into notification drama.; SH04-SH05 as one 4.5 s take, which chains a timed drip, a ±1 cm landing, a cued buzz, readable rings and a dead-still hand. Method: practical, locked, with the settle join as the fallback. Acceptance: no jump in the hand, the mug, the steam, the phone or the dripper. The rack, her torso and L never enter frame. The steam stays as thin threads, with none added. Risk: a visible join, or heavy steam that reads as stock lifestyle footage.

### S4

- **performance beats**: The product's scene, with no person and no performance. Hero beat: the whole SH06 hold on PROP-01, with the thickest arc of its glaze pool facing the lens: the mark her thumb crossed in SH02A. Nothing in frame moves (script).

Real detail: the phone is still face-down exactly where she flipped it, and its mug-side edge now meets bare oak where the mug stood. She took the coffee and left the phone (world bible S4; ruling 7). If SH06's readability test shows only the case corner, the detail becomes the case corner beside bare oak. Mark 2 is texture only, and it is outside this frame.

Taste note 'The product is a tool, then a hero': the dripper worked in S1-S3 and closes the spot, with no logo card.

Entry: PROP-01 ST5 (reset exactly on 232.7/280, filter out, empty, outer glaze dry). PROP-02 ST4. PROP-03 gone. PROP-07 ST2. PROP-08 plugged in, off frame. No steam. PROP-06 cools off frame and is heard only.
Exit: unchanged.
- **blocking**: Nobody is in frame. PROP-01 stands at 232.7/280, inside the clear zone (x 221.7-243.7, y 269-291, 0-25 cm above the top), with its front at 235° facing the lens. PROP-02 lies face-down and dark at 230.0/302.8, 8 cm outside the clear zone. The mug's spot (232.7/295) is bare oak between them.

SH06: one locked frame at C5 on 55°, about 25 cm across at the dripper's plane (ruling 8). It replaces the earlier plan of a 34 cm B5 plus a B6 cut-in. The dripper sits 3-4 cm right of centre. On plan:
- the bare mug footprint is about 12 cm frame-left of the dripper;
- the case's mug-side edge enters about 13 cm frame-left of the dripper and runs out past the frame edge;
- the old contact point (232.7/299) is only about 1.5 cm inside the frame edge.
Script B5-B6 keep the phone at the frame edge, so at least 3 cm of the case stays in under both cards. PROP-04 is well frame-right, out of frame.
Cards and the sign-off sit in the world bible's bare card area on the wall-D paint (125 cm up and above), clear of the dripper and the case edge.

SH08 (D02-2): the same dripper mark on a separate 4:5 plate, with PROP-02 and PROP-08 struck and bare oak around it. It stays bare as the DTC plate (ruling 7).
- **action progression**: SH06, B5-B6 (11.5-15.0): one locked hold of 84 f at 24 fps.
- 'Hand-glazed stoneware.' runs from 11.5 to 13.0 s.
- 'Shop the Solace dripper.' runs from 13.0 to 15.0 s.
- The 'Solace' sign-off runs from 11.5 to 15.0 s, across the card change.
Only the cards change; nothing in the picture moves. SH08 is the same state as a still.
- **emotional shift**: Hold. There is no trigger: nothing moves and nobody is there. The turn landed in the cut: the coffee went with her, and the phone stayed. The held frame lets it land.
- **transition in**: A same-axis 1.7x cut-in at 11.5 s from SH05 (see S3 transition_out). The eye lands on the dripper right of centre, with bare oak where the mug stood and the case edge at frame left.
- **transition out**: There is no internal cut: the card change at 13.0 s is the only change. The spot ends on a hard out on the held frame at 15.0 s, with no fade and no logo card (taste reject). Room tone and the kettle's tick run to the last frame. If the platform loops, the cut back to SH01's lit, face-up phone reads as the next 7:00.
- **campaign idea link**: 'Before the phone, the inbox, or anyone else gets a piece of you, there are five minutes that are only yours — marked by the pour.' Carried by SH06: PROP-01 at rest beside the bare oak where the mug stood and the edge of the face-down PROP-02, so the spent marker of the pour sits next to the phone she refused. If the SH06 test shows only the case corner, the link is instead PROP-01 beside the face-down case corner and bare oak, and the cut test is unchanged. Cut this and the viewer loses the readable closing product moment (a must-include), the glaze pool that shows 'hand-glazed', the CTA and the last sight of the refused phone. They would know the ritual but not what to buy.
- **practical or compositing flags**: claim_demo_needs_clearance. SH06 and SH08 demonstrate the verified fact 'Hand-glazed stoneware' through the uneven glaze pool. Method: a practical packshot on the client's real unit, with no retouch that adds, moves or exaggerates the pooling. The world bible's glaze, colour and dimensions are ASSUMED, and no reference has been inspected. Acceptance: the pool on screen matches the physical unit side by side, and the clear zone is free of drops, grounds, fingerprints and crumbs. Risk: a generated or painted pool is fabricated proof of hand-glazing.; Readability of the closing product moment. Method: a real-unit test at C5, on the camera plan's frame and its tighter test variant, viewed on a 6-inch phone at 100% with both cards on. Acceptance: the pool's thickest 4 cm arc reads under both cards. The bare mug footprint and the case's mug-side edge read together as 'where the mug stood, next to the phone', with at least 3 cm of the case in. No card overlaps the dripper or the case edge. If the footprint and the edge don't read together, the ruled frame stays, and the link and the real detail switch to the case corner beside bare oak, as already written. Risk: the must-include fails, or the CTA plays over a packshot with no idea in it.; A locked 84-frame hold with nothing moving. Method: a practical plate, not a generated clip. Acceptance: no shimmer, drift or boiling on the product from 11.5 to 15.0 s, and the phone stays dark. Risk: drift reads as synthetic.; Reflections in the gloss pool and the rim band. Method: practical; how they are controlled is the Cinematographer's call. Must show: only the bare flats, CEIL-01 and TILE-01's edge (the world bible's mirror paths), plus at most an unreadable dark shape. Must not show: crew, rig or a light. No retouch on the pool. Risk: a reflected rig in the DTC image can't be painted out without touching the claim.; Wetness only grows. Method: practical. For SH06 the cone interior keeps the faint damp sheen it had after the brew, and the outer glaze is checked dry before each take. SH08 is a separate plate and can be fully dry. Acceptance: no drops, grounds or fibres, and dry outer glaze. Risk: a bone-dry interior breaks continuity, and drops on the outside read as a leak.; Supers. Method: composite (the editor's). Acceptance: no card or sign-off ever overlaps the dripper or covers the case edge at frame left. Risk: text over the product costs the readable closing moment, and text over the phone costs the idea.

## Sound

| Cue | For | Type | When | What we hear | Rights |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SND-D01-001 | D01 | atmosphere | D01-B1 to D01-B6, 0.00-15.00 s (f0-f360 @ 24 fps) | Room tone for NORA's kitchen: a small city flat at 7 a.m., heard close. What you hear: a fridge compressor's low, steady hum off screen (placed to match the world bible layout), and a thin wash of street traffic through a closed window, far off, with no horns or sirens. What's left out: birdsong (the dawn cliché); neighbours, TV or radio (nothing else in the room asks anything of her, so there's no background speech to clear); a clock tick (the phone's 7:00 is the only clock). It runs at one level, unbroken across every cut, and never drops to digital silence. The script's 'silence' is this tone on its own: B1 0.79-1.25 s (f19-f30, from the buzz cut to the pad knock); B1 1.25-1.50 s, running into B2 to 1.88 s (f30-f45, from the pad knock to the rack knock); B4b 10.00-11.50 s, running into B5 to 11.79 s (f240-f283, from the buzz stop to the first kettle tick). It also fills every other gap between the hits in 002-007. The longest are 3.00-4.70 s (f72-f113, from the seat-click ring-out to the kettle tip, under the B2 card and across the B2/B3 cut) and 8.46-9.21 s (f203-f221, from the set-down to the B4b buzz). Arc: the loudest moment is the B4b buzz, 9.21-10.00 s (f221-f240), where the face-down phone chatters on its four corner pads on the oak and ticks against the mug (006). The quietest is the tone alone straight after it (f240-f283), where the refusal lands. Muted viewers miss nothing: the 7:00 screen, the coffee rings, the glow and the cards carry the story. Feeds loop, so the level at 15.00 s matches 0.00 s and runs cleanly into the B1 buzz. Every D01 shot is a practical plate. Its production sound is a guide track only, nothing from it is cut in, and every cue is rebuilt from the foley session. | Preferred source: a wild room-tone track recorded for this production at the foley session, in a real apartment kitchen. - The phone reads as a diamond at 19-58% w, 38-63% h. '7:00' sits at about 39% w, 48% h, leaning about 27° frame-right (it was 42.5° from the old 55°/50° position).
- The mug with its tide line sits at 58-83% w, 40-58% h.

- The rack is out, about 43 cm frame-right (ruling 1).
- R's forearm enters from the bottom edge left of centre and exits frame-right past the mug's near side. Her elbow stays low at her side at the counter edge (about 108 cm), which keeps it just under the bottom edge. A raised elbow brings the upper arm in at bottom-left.
ARC:
- B1, 65mm at 1.1 m: 5:1, and the lit phone is the hardest accent.
- B2, 65mm to 50mm: 5:1, and cream is the brightest detail.
- B3, the longest lens at the closest distance (100mm at 1.0 m): 4:1 once the phone is dark.
- B4, back to 65mm at 1.39 m, the widest frame: 3:1 so the rings read.
- S4 hero, 100mm at 1.25 m: 3:1.
Camera height falls from +31 (C1) through +29, +22, +17 and +15 to +7 at C5. Every shot is locked.
- **lens intent**: 65mm at 1.1 m (34 cm wide), f/11, focused at 1.06 m, so about 0.98-1.16 m is sharp. Sharp: the phone glass, and the mug rim with its tide line. Nearest soft: the forearm at the bottom edge. Reason: tight enough to read '7:00' (digits ASSUMED 2.4 cm, about 65-75 px tall on a 1080-wide frame) and wide enough to hold the mug.
- **camera movement**: Locked off on a high arm. Every shot in the plan is locked (route departure, blocker 5).
- **lighting**: Key: a unit behind WIN-01's diffusion frame, imitating the 7:00 window (declared), from frame-left, three-quarter back. It runs at 4400K against a 5400K WB (about 1/4 CTO warm), set for f/11. Fill: a passive aisle bounce frame-right, 5:1 on the oak. The phone field is the brightest detail: +1 stop over the oak, unclipped for the comp, about 6500K. Its cool spill lights the landing hand from below, against the warm window edge.
- **action**: 0-14f: the phone lies face-up with '7:00' lit, buzzing in its footprint. 14f: R enters from the bottom edge, and the digits stay clear until it lands (about 12 cm in frame, 0.58 m/s). 19f: it lands, thumb on the wall-C long edge and fingertips on the mug-side edge; the buzz stops. 19-29f: it lifts the phone 2 cm, rolls it over the long axis toward the mug and lets go 1 cm up. 29f: the case knocks the oak and comes to rest face-down, touching the mug. 29-36f: R carries on frame-right past the mug. Cut on the move.
- **performance**: Habit speed onto the phone and off it: no hover, no glance, one roll. The elbow stays low at her side. Only the hand, forearm and 5-15 cm of sleeve show.
- **board note**: READ: '7:00' is the brightest thing in frame. The one banner under it is too small to read, so the buzz is someone wanting her, not an alarm. The digits read clear for 19 frames. At 19f the hand lands and the buzz stops under it. One roll puts the phone face-down against the mug, with no look and no pause, so the first thing we see her do is refuse. By 29f the phone is dark and the hand is already moving on past the mug. TEXTURE (not a read): the tide line inside the empty mug (S1's detail) and the cable's run at upper left. CUT after f35: a straight cut with screen direction matched. The hand leaves frame-right, and SH02A picks it up entering frame-left, moving the same way, already at the rack (new information: where the hand was going). Her walk to the rack falls in the cut, as the script's B1 decision sets it.
- **sound cues**: SND-D01-001 from f0: room tone, unbroken; alone f0-f1, f19-f29, f31-f35, running on into SH02A; SND-D01-002 from f2: one pulse f2-f19, stopping on f19 as the hand lands (picture: buzzing from 0f, stops 19f; in tolerance); pad knock f30 (picture knock 29f; in tolerance); SND-D01-008 from f0: no music; SND-D01-009 from f0: no VO
- **entry state**: NORA: hips at N2 (x 195, y 308), out of frame; R empty, below the bottom edge left of centre; L off frame (not placed by the camera plan). PROP-02 (ST1): face-up at 230.0/304.8, top toward 90°; '7:00' and one unreadable banner lit (composite on the blank practical field); buzzing; mug-side edge 2 cm from the mug. PROP-08 (ST1): plugged in at 222.3/304.8; loop x 210-218, y 308-318; run along y 322 to the grommet at 258/322. PROP-03 (ST1): empty at 232.7/295, handle 90°, chip 195°, tide line 2 cm below the rim. PROP-07 (ST1): clean oiled oak. Off frame: PROP-01 (ST1) upright in the rack at 232.7/213, dry, empty, front 235°; PROP-04 (ST1) about 43 cm frame-right; PROP-05 (ST1) in the upper cabinet; PROP-06 (ST1) heating on the burner at 212/97.
- **exit state**: NORA: hips at N2; R empty, moving frame-right past the mug's near side toward wall A, elbow low at her side, leaving frame on the cut; no look; L off frame. PROP-02 (ST2): face-down and dark at 230.0/302.8, mug-side edge touching the mug at 232.7/299; still, no edge glow. PROP-03 (ST1): empty and unmoved, phone against it at 0°. PROP-07 (ST1). Off frame: PROP-01 (ST1) in the rack; PROP-04 (ST1); PROP-05 (ST1) in the cabinet; PROP-06 (ST1) heating.

- **camera movement**: Locked off.
- **lighting**: Key: the WIN-01 unit, far off, from frame-left back, 4400K against a 5400K WB, 5:1. A flag takes the white plate 1 stop down so the cream dripper is the brightest detail. Separation: cream against the grey tray and the oak, by value.
- **action**: 0-6f: R comes in from frame-left (about 15 cm, easing from 0.6 m/s or less), and the index and middle fingers hook the near rim. 6-14f: as the grip closes, the thumb lands at the foot and sweeps up across the pool to the rim band without stopping (about 9 cm at about 0.27 m/s). 14-24f: R lifts the dripper 14 cm, and the foot clears the 13 cm wires at about 22f (average 0.34 m/s, peak 0.55 or less), with a faint knock off the tray. From 21f the path bends frame-left and away into the set-off, leaving the cut about 3 cm into it at about 0.3 m/s. About 67 cm of the 82 cm carry falls in the cut.
- **performance**: She takes it like an everyday mug, and the thumb doesn't stop. The front stays at 235°, with no turn in the hand.
- **board note**: READ: the first look at the rack and at the product, held 24f (1.0 s). The eye finds the cream dripper, the brightest thing in frame now that the plate is flagged down. It stands in the rack beside a plate and a tumbler: it lives here like a mug, in daily use. The hand hooks the rim, and the thumb sweeps up over the glaze pooled at the foot without stopping. At phone size this reads as a grip, not a caress, and it doesn't need to read as more, because B5 names what the thumb touched. The card lands in the top band (16-26% h) at f0, and the far rim peaks 3% under it. TEXTURE: the dry plate and tumbler, and TILE-01's bullnose end behind the card (camera plan blocker 3). CUT after f23: an elliptical cut mid-carry as the dripper bends frame-left and away. About 67 cm of the carry and her walk back to N2 fall in the cut. SH02B picks the dripper up still moving right to left, and the 30° change of angle hides the skipped travel.
- **on screen text**: f0-f23: Nobody gets you before the pour.
- **sound cues**: SND-D01-001 from f0: room tone; alone f0-f8, f12-f23, running on across the cut; SND-D01-003 from f9: rack-tine knock f9-f12 (picture: the lift starts 14f and the foot clears the wires about 22f; see SYNC); SND-D01-008 from f0: no music; SND-D01-009 from f0: no VO

### Contradictions the storyboard flagged

- CLAIM · SH02A-SH08 (every panel that shows PROP-01). The world bible still reads 'Shape and size are ASSUMED ... no dimensions, colour or finish were supplied', so the handle-less cone, the cream glaze, the speck, the rim band, the foot pool and the raw foot are all assumed. Only 'Hand-glazed stoneware', 'Dishwasher safe' and 'Made in small batches' are verified. The pool now carries more of the spot. The script's decision makes the SH02A thumb pass the in-use demonstration of 'hand-glazed', B5, B6 and D02-2 turn the pool to the lens, and camera plan SH06 and SH08 build their lighting around its upper fillet. The SH02B seat depends on the assumed raw band hanging 2 mm outside the mug rim. What breaks: if the real unit has no pool or a different foot, the thumb pass, both hero frames and the DTC image show a feature the product lacks, and that is a visual claim nobody has confirmed. Owner: producer (production sample and brand sign-off), then the copywriter, cinematographer and production designer. Fix: supply a brand-confirmed real-sample reference before any product frame is generated, dressed or shot. If there is no pool, the copywriter re-picks the B2/B5/B6/D02-2 mark and the cinematographer relights SH06 and SH08. If the foot differs, the SH02B seat is re-planned. The panels mark every pool and foot read ASSUMED. Also a producer blocker. The Mark 1 ring part of last round's flag is gone, because the bible now keeps the work zone clean.
- ROUTE CAMERA · SH01-SH08. Route R1's visual_language asks for a 'handheld-but-steady camera', but the camera plan locks every shot ('route departure, blocker 5'). What breaks: the route outranks the camera plan, and the departure isn't approved. The SH04-SH05 single take, both same-axis cut-ins (SH02B-SH03, SH05-SH06) and the '7:00' composite all depend on the lock. Owner: cinematographer, for Lucas. Fix: Lucas approves the locked grammar, or the plan is redone handheld-but-steady and the board with it. Boarded locked. Also a producer blocker.
- ROUTE LIGHT · SH08. Route R1 asks for 'warm low window light'. Camera plan SH08 balances neutral (a 5000K key on a 5000K WB), as 'a deliberate difference from D01's warm balance'. The script now serves D02-2 alone as the DTC image, but it still pairs with a warm D02-1. What breaks: a route departure that Lucas hasn't approved. Owner: cinematographer, for Lucas. Fix: Lucas approves the neutral DTC still, or the cinematographer also shoots SH08 warm so Lucas can choose on the plates. Boarded neutral. Also a producer blocker.
- SYNC (re-opened on a new root cause) · SH02A, SH02B, SH03, SH04, SH05. As the reviewers asked, I re-derived every cue line from the re-synced sound plan. But the sound plan was re-synced to the superseded camera plan. Its hits still assume SH02B from f54, SH04 from f180 and SH05 from f216, while the current plan starts them at f60, f168 and f204 (SH02A is now 24f, SH03 60f). Against the current action frames (in-shot frames, absolute in brackets): (1) The SH02A rack knock is at 9-12f [f45-f48], but the lift starts at 14f and the foot clears the wires at about 22f [f50-f58], so the knock sounds before the dripper moves. (2) The SH02B seat click is at 7f [f67], but the picture click is at 14f [f74], as the camera plan states, so it sounds 7f early, mid-carry. (3) In SH03 the kettle tip is at 5f [f113] and the stream lands at 7f [f115], but the spout only arrives and tips at 10f [f118], so the water is heard about 5f before it's poured. (4) The SH04 last drip is at 21f [f189], but the drop is at 4f, window 4-10f [f172-f178], so it sounds 17f late, during the carry. (5) The SH04 set-down is at 35f [f203], but the landing is at about 20f [f188], so it sounds 15f late, as the pads reach the mug. (6) The SH05 pulse runs 17f-36f [f221-f240], though the plan's own label reads 'SH05 5f-24f'. The picture lights and rattles at 5f-24f [f209-f228], and the script stops the rattle and brings the card up at 9.5 s [f228], so the buzz starts 12f after the rings and runs 12f past the card-in. Every SH01 and SH06 hit is inside ±2f, and so is the SH03 stream break (target 53f [f161], against the 48-53f tip-back). The 003 alternate crinkle (f99-f105) has no picture, because SH02B has no filter action (script, bible). What breaks: six hits sit outside ±2f, and in SH05 the turn's sound trails its own picture. Owner: sound designer. The cinematographer confirms the SH02A contact frame: the camera plan has 'a faint knock off the tray', the sound plan 'one coated-wire tine'. Fix: put the rack knock on the take's contact frame within f50-f58. Move the seat click to f74 (ring gone by f79), the kettle tip to f118 and the stream landing to the take, about f119. Put the last drip at f172, adjusted to the take within f172-f178, and the set-down on the take's landing frame, about f188. Run the 006 pulse at f209-f228 and delete the alternate crinkle. Then re-derive 001's tone-alone windows (the quietest stretch becomes f228-f283) and its loudest-moment timing. Boarded the sound plan's frames, with the picture frame noted in each cue line.
- CARD 7:05 · SH05. Script B4b says 'The card comes up at 9.5s as the rings flatten', which is SH05 24f [f228]. Camera plan SH05's action says 'By about 30f (9.75 s) the rings are flat as the card comes up', 6f later. What breaks: an editor cutting from the camera plan lands the card 6f late, after the rings have settled rather than as they flatten. Owner: cinematographer. Fix: conform the SH05 action note to the script's 24f. Boarded the script's f24.
- RING NAME · SH04, SH05, SH07, SH08, and the SH01 marks list. The camera plan still calls 232.7/280 'the ring' ('Dripper on the ring', 'the ring is clean oak', 'the foot is on the ring mark'), a name left over from last round's Mark 1 stain. The world bible now keeps the work zone clean: 'no rings, stains, drip spots or knife marks, so no frame shows a mark the product made'. What breaks: a dressing or generation pass that reads 'ring mark' adds a coffee ring under the foot, and on the DTC image (SH08) that suggests a dripper that drips or stains. Owner: cinematographer. Fix: rename it 'set-down mark 232.7/280' throughout. Boarded as the set-down mark on clean oak.
- B6 ALT RATE · SH06. The script's organic alternate B6 card, 'Shop the Solace dripper. Link in bio.', is 37 characters in B6's 2.0 s (18.5 cps), with the 'Solace' sign-off beneath it. B6 ran 2.5 s last round, and every other card now reads at 15.5 cps or less (paid B6: 12.0). What breaks: on the organic cut, the last card may not finish reading before the loop. Owner: copywriter. Fix: confirm the alternate passes the script's timing check at 2.0 s, or trim it. Boarded with paid as primary and the alternate noted.
- CLOSED THIS ROUND, with the department that resolved each. RACK: the copywriter (B1's hand now carries on toward a rack out of frame, and the script's decision is that the rack is first seen in B2's first angle) and the production designer (the B1 lock is now 02/03/08). B2 PICTURE: the copywriter (B2 is rewritten as two angles with a cut mid-carry, the card clear of the dripper in both, and 'lower half' is gone). S2 DETAIL: the copywriter (a script decision declares SH03's break of 'One real detail per scene' on purpose, and D02-1 carries the scorch band and the pouring hand), recorded by the production designer in the bible. S4 DETAIL: the production designer (S4's detail is now the phone left face-down with its mug-side edge meeting bare oak, which sits in SH06's frame-left wedge; Mark 2 is texture only; D02-2 stays bare on purpose as the DTC image). FIRST LOOK: the cinematographer (SH02A is now 24f = 1.0 s). This holds only while the director accepts camera plan blocker 6 over ruling 6's 18f, and it reopens if 18f stands. ELLIPSIS: the copywriter (the walk to the rack falls across the B1/B2 cut by script decision) and the cinematographer (SH02A's entry is 'matching SH01's exit'); boarded as a direction-matched cut across the walk. FRAME CONTENTS: the production designer (the B3 lock adds PROP-02's wall-D end with PROP-08 off frame, and the B5-B6 lock is 02 only with PROP-08 off frame) and the cinematographer (PROP-08 is dropped from SH03's prop_ids); all eight locks now match the prop_ids. OUT-01: the production designer (OUT-01 moved below the counter top, in no frame; PROP-08 re-routed along y 322 and down the grommet at 258/322, with nothing running up wall D), matched by the cinematographer's frames: no outlet in SH02B, SH04, SH05 or SH08, and no cable in a card zone. POURING HAND (film director's note): SH03 and SH07 now pour with L, with R at her side off frame, conformed to the camera plan and casting. GRIP (all three review notes): SH04's exit, SH05's entry and exit, and the NORA and PROP-03 tracked states now read 'R finger pads along the mug's aisle side over 225-315°, below the rim, palm clear, heel on the oak at 225.7/295' (casting B4b, bible PROP-03 ST4, script B4b), and SH05's risk line adds that a full-palm wrap or grip fails the take. SYNC could not be closed (see SYNC). The earlier closures (HAND, BUZZ COUNT, B5 CARD, CARD ZONE, MARKS, FRONT BEARING, THUMB, SH07 RING) stay closed.
- Checks run: (1) Shot IDs: 9 panels for the 9 camera-plan shots, in order (SH01, SH02A, SH02B, SH03, SH04, SH05, SH06, SH07, SH08). (2) Timing: the D01 shots sum to 36+24+48+60+36+72+84 = 360f = 15.0 s. The beats map as B1 = SH01 (f0-f36); B2 = SH02A (f36-f60) + SH02B (f60-f108), with the card across the cut; B3 = SH03 (f108-f168); B4a = SH04 (f168-f204); B4b = SH05 (f204-f276); B5 = SH06 f0-f35 (f276-f312); B6 = SH06 f36-f83 (f312-f360). (3) Sound frames: every sound-plan absolute frame was converted to in-shot frames and checked against the camera plan's action frames at ±2f, with six misses (SYNC). The SH01 pulse start (f2 against picture 0f) and knock (f30 against 29f) are in tolerance. The SH06 ticks clear the card-ins by at least 7f. (4) Copy: every D01 card, the sign-off and both stills' headlines and sublines (paid and organic) match the script word for word. None sits over the product, and each sits in the camera plan's named zone. Card rates: B2 10.7 cps (32 characters in 3.0 s), B4b 15.5 (31 in 2.0 s), B5 14.7 (22 in 1.5 s), B6 paid 12.0 (24 in 2.0 s), B6 organic 18.5 (37 in 2.0 s; B6 ALT RATE). VO: 0 words (SND-D01-009). (5) Cues: every cue ID exists in the sound plan (001-009), and none was invented. Silence is boarded as 001's tone-alone windows. (6) Continuity: entry equals the previous exit at SH04-SH05 (one take). The marked cuts are SH01-SH02A (direction-matched, the walk in the cut, scripted), SH02A-SH02B (mid-carry ellipsis, scripted), and the S2, S3 and S4 time jumps into SH03, SH04 and SH06. NORA's position and both hands are written into every state, and L is placed only where the camera plan places it. (7) First looks: every shot holds at least 24f. (8) Frame contents and marks: the bible's eight frame locks match the prop_ids. The marks were checked against the bible: phone ST1 and ST2, mug, set-down mark, rack, grommet and cable route, and the burner at 212/97 (the sound plan pans from 214/100, within 4 cm). (9) Mirror checks: the mole (R) in SH01, SH02A, SH02B, SH04 and SH05; the scar (L) in SH07. (10) Claims: all copy was checked against the 3 verified facts. Every pool and foot read is marked ASSUMED, and SH06, SH07 and SH08 are real unit only because they prove a verified fact. (11) Taste notes: no face; no window (SH07's sill lock is pending the frame test); no music; no slow motion (SH03 plays at 100%, and SH07 is a strobe still); the product is in the last frame; a rack and a cable are in frame. 'One real detail per scene': S1's tide line (SH01); S2's declared break (SH03), with the scorch band in SH07; S3's rings (SH05); S4's phone left behind (SH06); SH08 bare on purpose (production designer). (12) Sound-off read: the '7:00' screen, the rings and the cards carry the story, so no caption is missing. (13) Review notes: the grip wording, the pouring hand, the cue lines and each named flag were addressed as listed under CLOSED THIS ROUND and SYNC.
