## Assumptions and blockers

### Strategy

- No research or reference_reader tool was bound in this run; all audience and behavioral reasoning above is the strategist's own inference from the supplied brief, not sourced market or audience research. All such reasoning is labeled as hypothesis in content.hypotheses, not fact.
- The brief's references array is empty, so no reference asset was available or treated as visually inspected in producing this strategy.
- Solace Home Goods is confirmed fictional/a test brand; nothing here implies a real company, real product performance, or claims beyond the three verified_product_facts supplied.
- No real person's likeness, casting, or availability is addressed at this stage — that is out of scope for the strategist role and belongs to casting_director per the studio workflow.
- Numeric success targets are intentionally left unset (see content.success_criteria) since the brief and upstream context gave no baseline figures or target percentages; setting real targets requires producer/brand input before use as an approval gate.
- This artifact is proposed strategist output per the studio's shared_context rules ("Workers read an immutable task snapshot and return proposed artifacts") and is expected to be validated by the producer and built on by the creative_director at stage 03_concepts before any human approval gate.

### Routes

- No real people are depicted or implied: any on-camera hands/figures in the execution examples are fictional, generic talent concepts, not a real individual's likeness — casting_director will formalize this in the casting_bible at the next stage.
- No reference imagery was supplied with this brief (references: [] in the project record) and no reference_reader tool is bound in this run, so none was or could be visually inspected; the visual-language descriptions above are original creative direction, not derived from any inspected reference asset.
- On-screen/closing lines quoted in the execution examples (e.g., 'Made in small batches', 'A five-minute ritual, on purpose') are illustrative placeholders to show how the required closing product moment could read; final wording is the copywriter's deliverable at the next stage, not locked copy.
- Assumed the depicted moment is a solitary, at-home morning scenario (person alone, no household/family context), consistent with the brief's 'five-minute ritual... on purpose' framing and the strategist's audience read — this is a creative choice, not a stated brief requirement.
- Assumed a single named location/setup (home kitchen counter, window light) is sufficient for a :15 spot and two stills; no additional location was specified in the brief.
- Treated 'made in small batches' and 'hand-glazed' as visually demonstrable through texture/variation in-camera, and 'dishwasher safe' as better suited to supporting copy than to any on-screen visual, since it is not something a viewer can see in a shot.

### Script

- FLAG TO SOUND: the B5/B6 boundary moves from 12.5 to 13.0s and the sign-off now starts at 11.5s, so the kettle-tick clearance in SND-D01-007 needs checking again. That call belongs to sound, not the script.
- FLAG TO DESIGN AND THE PRODUCER: ART-LOCK-0700 changes. The B1 lock screen now shows 7:00 with one generic notification banner beneath it, its text too small to read, and no app name, icon, date or status-bar text.
- FLAG TO THE DIRECTOR: B2's first angle has to show the thumb crossing the glaze at the dripper's foot as the hand closes. How that is framed is the director's call.
- The filter is pressed in and the grounds are spooned in off screen, in the B2/B3 cut. If the director wants the filter on screen, it goes into B2's second angle from a stack in front of the mug so the hand never reaches over the phone, and the pace has to be checked again.
- The B5 card is 'Hand-glazed stoneware.' alone. Stacking all three facts with the CTA would put 13 words on screen.
- With the sign-off, the organic B6 frame holds 8 words ('Shop the Solace dripper. Link in bio.' plus 'Solace'). I treat the sign-off as a brand mark, not card copy. If the 7-word ceiling is read to include it, the organic B6 drops the sign-off, because its card already names Solace.
- No logo artwork was supplied. The 'Solace' sign-off is set as type until the brand supplies a lockup.
- The product is called 'the Solace dripper', following the route. The name has to be confirmed before the end card and the sublines are locked.
- '7:00' and '7:05' dramatise the key message ('A five-minute ritual...') and are not a brew-time claim. If legal reads them as a performance claim: the B4b card becomes 'Everyone else can have you later.' The D02-2 headline becomes 'Out for the pour. Back after.', which still holds both ends of the window without clock times. The B1 lock screen keeps 7:00, which is just the time of day.
- The B4b rings are assumed to come from the phone's vibration passing through the mug, with the fingertip grip. If they don't show on the day, production reinforces the vibration practically. A card or VO must not stand in for them.
- Off-screen actions: the filter and grounds go in during the B2/B3 cut. The mug and the filter are removed between B4b and B5.
- The dripper is assumed to sit directly on a mug with a paper filter. If it needs a different filter or a carafe, B2 to B4b have to change.
- Hero-unit fallback (PROP-01): if the hero unit has no glaze pool at the foot, its speck and the thin break at the rim become the mark. The thumb crosses it in B2, and it faces the lens in B5, B6 and D02-2. If the unit shows neither, the whole legible dripper plus the B5 card carries the fact.
- The $38 price is not on the verified-facts list, so it stays off screen unless the client approves it.
- RIGHT, PENDING: a hand-performer release for the performer whose hands and forearms play NORA, in the spot and in still 1. Scope: organic and paid social, 9:16 video and 4:5 stills; term and territory TBD. Owner: producer. NORA is fictional and uses no real person's name. Not cleared.
- RIGHT, NOT CURRENTLY NEEDED: a voice-performer release, because there is no VO. If VO is added, the release needs the same scope. Owner: producer.
- RIGHT, PENDING: the B1 lock-screen art (ART-LOCK-0700). It needs a de-branded handset and a generic mock-up showing only 7:00 and one unbranded banner with unreadable text. Owner: producer. Not cleared.
- RIGHT, PENDING: the kettle, the mug and any other prop in frame must be unbranded or cleared. Owner: producer. Not cleared.
- RIGHT, PENDING: sound effects, recorded on set or licensed for the same scope. Owner: producer, with sound. Not cleared.
- No reference imagery was supplied or inspected, and no reference_reader tool is bound in this run.

### Cast

- NORA is the only character the script names. The beat sheet lists her in S1-S3. S4, D01-B5, D01-B6 and D02-2 have no person in them. No one else is cast or added.
- No face is cast, because the script shows NORA as hands and forearms only. If an edit adds her face, casting reopens to set face anchors, so the generator never sets them by default.
- NORA is fictional and not modelled on any real person. She is not presented as a customer or an expert, and there is no testimonial. No reference imagery was supplied or inspected. Nothing here clears the rights to make her, whether generated or live (see blockers).
- Skin lock: 'medium-deep, warm undertone' goes with #6F4A34 (back of the hand and forearm) and 'medium, warm undertone' with #B3886A (palms and fingertip pads). These phrases apply in the style bible, the hand-double call and every prompt. They are my reading of the hex values, not a measured match. A live hand double is matched by eye under the shoot's key light.
- Changes to the anchors this round. The writer's callus is now backstory only, because most grips press it against the index finger, so it cannot hold continuity. The raised veins are now unlocked age texture in screen_presence, because heat and hand height change them. A new permanent mark, the left-hand scar, gives the pouring hand its own anchor, the way the mole marks the right hand.
- Two review notes are reconciled here. The film director's note wants the left-handed pour on record, with a left-handed test at the hand-double call. The later note wants the per-shot hand list and the kettle-handle geometry cut from casting. So the left-handed pour appears once, in wardrobe_fit_notes, as something the performer must be able to do, and its test is in the live-performer blocker. Which hand does what in each shot, and where the kettle handle sits, belong to the director's shot list. The 0.9 L gooseneck size comes from the director's note, not from the script. Casting's reading is that a right-handed person pouring with the left hand is plausible as a daily habit, because of where the kettle lives.
- Every generated take is checked for the mark on the hand in use: the scar on the left, the mole on the right, never swapped. Generators default to a right-handed pour, so D02-1 is the take most at risk.
- The mug stays on the counter through B4b. The script's B5 opens 'moments later' with the mug and hand gone, so no lift is performed on screen.
- The market is assumed to be the US, based on the $ price and 'link in bio'. Coffee equipment is not age-restricted, so no code minimum applies.
- It is an assumption, not a verified fact, that the dripper comes in one size and has no handle. The hand-scale rule depends on the dripper's dimensions and the director's grip (see blockers). The 2 cm margin, about 1 cm of glaze showing on each side of the hand, is my number.
- Glaze separation. The glaze colour isn't specified; the route gives only the palette of terracotta, cream and soft grey. The risky case is terracotta, which sits close to #6F4A34 in both hue and value, so the hand could merge with the dripper at B2, B4a and D02-1. That would be a hand hiding the product through tone rather than size. The skin value stays fixed, because it is a casting choice with stated reasons: it breaks both the category's default hand and the generator's. If the client locks a terracotta glaze, separation at B2, B4a and D02-1 goes to the film director and the cinematographer as a named check (light, sleeve value, background). Owner: film director and cinematographer. Status: pending until the glaze is confirmed. A cream or soft grey glaze separates by value and needs only the sleeve rule.
- No human-review flags apply: no child, no disability or visible difference, no religious dress, no pregnancy, and no one aged 70 or over.
- Blocker (producer): Rights for a generated NORA are not cleared. → Source: the chosen generator's output-use terms, plus any AI-content labelling the platforms require for paid and organic social. Scope: D01 (15 s, 9:16) and D02-1 (4:5), on social and the DTC site, in the US. Owner: producer. Status: pending.
- Blocker (producer): A live hand performer, if one is used for any insert or for the D02-1 still, needs a release, a match to the anchors and a tested left-handed pour. → Source: a hand-model release. Scope: D01 and D02-1, on social and the DTC site, in the US, for a term the producer sets to match the media plan. The performer must be 21 or older and match all five anchors. Skin is called in words: 'medium-deep, warm undertone' (#6F4A34, back of the hand) and 'medium, warm undertone' (#B3886A, palms), matched by eye under the shoot's key light. The right-hand mole and the left-hand scar may be applied by makeup (joint lock, stylist). Tested at the casting call: (1) with the left hand, pour a steady, thin spiral from a 0.9 L gooseneck, filled as for the shoot, onto grounds in a dripper, with no wobble or correction in the stream; (2) hold the right fingertips still on a mug through a 3 s take while a phone buzzes against it. Owner: producer. Status: pending.
- Blocker (producer (request to the client, Solace Home Goods); film_director (grip call)): The client has not supplied the dripper's height, rim diameter, body diameter at the foot, whether it has a handle, the sizes sold or the glaze colour. The director has not yet named the B2/B4a grip. The hand-scale rule and the skin-to-glaze separation both depend on these. → The client confirms the height, the rim diameter, the body diameter at the foot, whether there is a handle, the sizes sold and the glaze colour. The director names the B2/B4a grip: over the rim, a side grip around the body, or on the handle. The threshold is then set against the dimension that grip lays the knuckles across. If it is under 10.5 cm, casting reopens the hand breadth before generation or a live casting call. If the glaze is terracotta, separation at B2, B4a and D02-1 goes to the film director and the cinematographer as a named check, and the skin value does not change. Owner: producer (client request) and film director (grip). Status: pending.

### World

- ASSUMED: USA, 2026, a pre-war rental, from the USD price and 'link in bio'. That locks NEMA 5-15 at OUT-01 (in no frame), a gas range (the source of the kettle's scorch band), a 12-hour '7:00' and a USB-C phone. No window view, signage, street or plates appear.
- LOC-01 is a studio BUILD that a small crew can put up next week (ASSUMED). It consists of the counter run with the sink cut-out, the wall-D backsplash with the upper cabinets and TILE-01, full-height bare flats on walls A, B and C (with the WIN-01 opening in C), and CEIL-01. The wall-A flat, the full-height wall-B flat and CEIL-01 were added this round because the glaze pool and the mug glaze mirror almost the whole room. At their size only tone masses read, so bare flats stand in for the appliances. RANGE-01, FRIDGE-01, FAUCET-01, DOOR-01 and the base cabinet at y 0-40 are placed in plan but not built.
- Screen direction is solved in the plan. With the run on wall D, the rack lies at frame right of the mug as seen from the aisle, so the D01-B2 carry runs right to left. The flop-safe dressing stays as a fallback.
- The script lands the phone against the mug and sets the dripper down on the far side of the mug (D01-B1, D01-B4a). That fixes the phone, the port and the cable route. The cable rule is 'never enters y < 302', and the cable drops through the grommet at 258/322 to OUT-01 below the top.
- Mark 1, Mark 1b and the secondary swap test are cut. From D01-B1 to D01-B4a they showed a stain and a drip spot the product had made on oiled wood, which is an unverified negative claim built on an ASSUMED raw foot, and hiding them needed a ±3 mm set-down that no take holds. 232.7/280 is clean oak in every state, and the glaze pool, which rests on the verified 'hand-glazed', is the only swap test. The D01-B4a set-down now needs ±1 cm, and props reset the exact mark for D01-B5 across the script's 'moments later'. Props drain the cone before the D01-B4a take, so the script's last drip is the only one.
- No filter is on the counter before D01-B3. The pre-opened filter is cut for two reasons. 'Started the ritual before the phone lit' contradicted the S1 turn ('her five minutes start here'). And its spot past the phone would have sent her hand over the phone, which the script rules out. The filter and grounds go in during the D01-B2/D01-B3 cut, as the script decides. The S1 detail is now PROP-03's tide line. The earlier question about a filter fetch inside D01-B2 is withdrawn.
- The oak deepens from #7A5A3E to #533C2B. That is one step past the review's suggested #5A4230, so the raw flange also clears 3:1 (3.1:1, where #5A4230 gives 2.8:1). The body moves from 4.5:1 to 7.3:1 on the oak and the pool from 2.3:1 to 3.8:1; the body stays at 3.7:1 on the backsplash and the raw band at 2.9:1 on the mug. The phone bumper moves from charcoal #3E4447 (1.0:1 on the new oak) to stone grey #77756F (2.2:1), so the S4 phone still reads and still sits below the product. The black cable drops to 1.4:1, also below the product. The seams, Mark 2 and Mark 3 are re-coloured to read on the darker oak.
- TILE-01 is a cool sage-grey #D5D8D2 rather than the review's #DCD6CA, because the bible reserves cream for the product. It keeps the same 3.6:1 on the paint. It stays empty, adds no lock, stops 5.5 cm below the reserved card area and never backs the dripper's outline for a lens at or above the rim (99.5 cm).
- The coffee mirror line and the pool's section path are computed from the plan: coffee at 98.5 cm, pool at 92 cm, cone wall 18° off vertical (ASSUMED). They were not measured. Lens placement stays the DP's, and the prelight check is in blockers.
- This department reserves the wall-D card area (paint from 125 cm up, y 240-340) for composited cards. Where the cards sit is the storyboard's call; if they need to sit lower, TILE-01 moves, not the card.
- The WIN-01 translight stays struck. The pool's wall-C flank sees the WIN-01 opening, but the key's diffusion frame fills it, so nothing beyond it can show. Reinstate the translight and its rights item only if the prelight finds something beyond the diffusion in a reflection.
- S4's real detail is the phone left face-down where she flipped it (script D01-B5: 'the phone lies face-down and dark'). Mark 2 is texture only. D02-2 stays bare on purpose as the DTC image.
- PROP-04 is dressed dry as the dripper's everyday resting spot, with no sponge, soap, suds, standing water or cutlery cup. No story line says the dripper is hand-washed, so the set never contradicts D02-2's 'it still goes in the dishwasher' (verified: dishwasher safe).
- The lock screen carries the script's one generic banner, too small to read, drawn as blank shapes with no glyphs.
- D01-B4b timing follows the script: the buzz, glow and rings run about 8.5-9.5 s, and the card comes up at 9.5 s.
- In D01-B4b the hand rests on finger pads only, on the glaze over 225-315° below the rim, palm clear, with the heel on the oak 3 cm in front of the mug. The script notes that a full wrap would damp the rings. The handle, chip and phone contact stay clear.
- D02-1 uses the D01 ST2 phone mark, unplugged, with PROP-07 in ST1. Now that the work zone carries no marks, no wipe or reset is needed.
- The front faces 235° in every state, so nobody turns the dripper in the hand.
- The phone bumper has 1.5 mm corner pads and open edges, which gives the D01-B4b glow a physical path. A leak of 5-15 mm is ASSUMED until the glow test. The rings carry the turn.
- PROP-01's look is ASSUMED: a size-02 handle-less cone, cream satin glaze pooling to gloss, a raw foot and no exterior mark. Every hex and contrast figure depends on it.
- Contrast ratios are WCAG relative luminance, computed from the stated hexes under an even key (18% grey card #767676). Light direction, phone light balance and gloss highlights are DP requests.
- Some dressing is not named in the script: the charging cable and grommet, the rack contents and TILE-01. The filter is binned and the kettle returned off-screen between beats.
- Every cross-reference is keyed to the script's scene_ids (S1-S4) and beat_ids (D01-B1 to D01-B6, D02-1, D02-2). No shot or sound IDs from other departments are cited, since none is in this department's upstream.
- No reference imagery was supplied or inspected, and no reference_reader tool is bound in this run. Only the verified facts are relied on. The set makes no performance claims and shows no price or award cards.
- Blocker (production_designer): The D01-B4b edge glow on the bumper case has not been measured. The 5-15 mm leak on all four open edges is ASSUMED. → Before the shoot day, with the DP: shoot the case face-down at 230.0/302.8 on a #533C2B sample board with the lock screen lit, under the D01-B4b lighting, and publish which edges leak and how far. The edge touching the mug will likely leak least. D01-B4b does not depend on this, because the rings carry the turn.
- Blocker (production_designer): The grommet route, OUT-01 below the top, TILE-01's coffee mirror line, CEIL-01's extent and the glaze pool's mirror paths have been checked in plan and section only, not on a monitor. → At prelight, with the DP, confirm on the monitor that: (1) the D01-B2 card area, D01-B4a to D01-B4b, D01-B5 to D01-B6 and D02-2 show no outlet or cable; (2) the D01-B4b coffee shows paint and TILE-01's 112 cm edge where the published line puts it, and the rings bend that edge; (3) in D01-B5 to D01-B6 and D02-2 the pool sees only oak, flats, CEIL-01, TILE-01 and the WIN-01 diffusion, with no lamp opening, studio or crew beyond the lens flag; (4) TILE-01 never backs the dripper's outline; (5) D01-B2's first angle stays on the wall-C side of y 185, or FAUCET-01 gets built. If the plug end shows in D01-B5 to D01-B6, swap to a right-angle USB-C plug turned toward wall C within 1.5 cm of the port and republish the route. If anything beyond the WIN-01 diffusion shows in a reflection, reinstate the translight with its rights clearance.
- Blocker (sound_designer): The script's sound lines for D01-B2, D01-B4b, D01-B5 and D01-B6 call for kitchen room tone and the kettle ticking as it cools. LOC-01 is a stage build, so stage tone is not kitchen tone. → Record wild room tone, and the cooling ticks of an enamel stovetop kettle, in a real pre-war US rental kitchen, with the kettle placed as RANGE-01 is (about 1.8 m toward wall A from the dripper). Or confirm that stage tone is acceptable.
- Blocker (producer): PROP-01 is invented: its shape, size, glaze colourway, raw foot and lack of a stamp are all ASSUMED. Every hex and contrast figure, the swap test and D02-2 (the DTC product image) depend on it. Pending item: the client's product reference (photos or CAD of the shipping glaze, the foot finish and any stamp). Source: Solace Home Goods. Owner: producer. → Get the reference before PROP-01 is locked and before D02-2 is generated; work can continue on the ASSUMED spec until then. When it arrives, re-run the 3:1 checks against the #533C2B oak and the #6B6E67 paint, and repaint the walls lighter if the glaze's relative luminance is below 0.2. If the foot is glazed, the raw band's 2.9:1 separation on the mug goes: re-solve the seat and keep the glaze pool as the swap test. Any stamp stays on the underside.

### Director's treatment

- Timings follow the revised script: B1 0-1.5, B2 1.5-4.5, B3 4.5-7.0, B4a 7.0-8.5, B4b 8.5-11.5, B5 11.5-13.0, B6 13.0-15.0, with the 7:05 card at 9.5 s. I retimed the review's figures to match: the S3-to-S4 cut-in moves from 11.0 to 11.5 s, SH06 goes from 96 f to 84 f, and the join moves from 9.0 to 8.5 s. The decisions themselves are unchanged.
- Package-review rulings recorded here. (1) The rack is out of SH01, which is about 34 cm across. (2) SH02A/SH02B are approved. The filter-collar fix at x≈241 is moot, because the revised script puts the filter in during the B2/B3 cut. (3) SH03 keeps the phone's dark end in frame, the paint-out fallback is withdrawn, and the S2 break is declared. (4) SH04-SH05 is one take; reopened only if the ring test fails at SH05's frame size. (5) The SH05 wrist crossing the port end is accepted. (6) SH02A's 18 f are accepted. (7) S4's real detail is the phone left where she flipped it, Mark 2 is texture, and SH08 stays bare. (8) SH06 is one locked hold of about 25 cm at C5. (9) The SH01 grip is confirmed as written. (10) The SH02A fallback is a pickup, not generation. (11) The wall-B camera-plan blocker is deleted.
- This round's changes. (1) B3's objective is now 'start the bloom from the centre out without the stream touching the paper', with one pass mark and a stated minimum. (2) B4a is rebalanced to 4/4/10/6/12 f: the release gets 6 f and the travel-and-settle 12 f. The drop ends the hold, the buzz is cued off the settle, and the card follows the rings within ±0.25 s of 9.5 s. (3) The SH02A card comes in at 1.5 s with a world position and a phone-size thumb test, with a 25 cm fallback. (4) SH05 gets a phone-size ring test, with a 30 cm reframe on 31-55° as the fallback. (5) SH06 gets a footprint-and-edge test, with a conditional link. (6) Other departments' method and the lens figures are removed.
- The latest note removes lens and distance figures, while an earlier note asked for the S2 break to cite '100mm at 1.0 m'. I give the reason as SH03's frame width, about 20 cm, which is the substance of that reason. Lens and distance stay with the camera plan.
- Treatment blockers closed as superseded: the wall-B camera plan; the SH01-SH02 pace problem (solved by SH02A/SH02B); the '7:00' read (solved by taking the rack out of SH01); and the B6 phone-edge plan of record (replaced by the single C5 hold).
- Shot IDs: SH01 = B1. SH02A/SH02B = B2 (18 + 54 f, seat on SH02B f15). SH03 = B3. SH04 = B4a. SH05 = B4b (a 30 cm variant only if blocker 2 fails). SH06 = B5-B6 (84 f at C5). SH07 = D02-1. SH08 = D02-2.
- No tool binding was given, so every uncut action is 5 s or less. Every hand and product plate is still practical, and every fallback is a pickup. The longest uncut take is SH04-SH05 at 4.5 s. The composites are the '7:00' lock screen, any match on the SH05 glow, and the supers.
- Hands: NORA is right-handed. R does SH01, SH02A, SH02B, SH04 and SH05. L pours in SH03 (seen only as the spout) and SH07, where its fingers are the one exception to 'L never in frame'. Both of R's product grips cross the rim, so casting's breadth check applies to the rim diameter.
- The prop marks are the world bible's final set. NORA's marks are mine: N2 is centred at y 308, facing 90°, and the rack mark is at y 215. The body figures (167 cm, right shoulder about 137 cm up) are ASSUMED. Lateral and depth figures are plan figures at counter height. The frame widths are targets, and lens, height and lighting are the Cinematographer's.
- Deliberate taste-note break: 'One real detail per scene' in S2. SH03 carries the pour alone, and the scorch band moves to SH07. Cutting the window shot is the script's own break from the route, and I follow it. No other taste note is broken.
- Hypotheses, not verified: (1) about 6-7 g of water (about 4 g/s for 1.6 s) visibly starts a bloom on 18-20 g of grounds at 100% speed; (2) the phone's buzz ripples the coffee through the phone-to-mug contact, with her hand resting on the mug; (3) a cone drained until drips slow to about one a second will have a drop hanging at 7.0 s. None of these is a product claim.
- Changes across the time cuts are ellipses, never off-screen hand-offs. S1 to S2: the filter and grounds go in, and L takes the kettle. S2 to S3: the kettle goes back and the coffee draws down. S3 to S4: the mug is taken, the filter binned, the steam gone, and the dripper reset.
- The fictional NORA is played by a hand double matched to the casting anchors. No real person's name, likeness or identity is used. The D02 stills are separate 4:5 plates, not crops of the native 9:16 D01.
- No reference was inspected, because no reference_reader is bound. The product's look, size and glaze are ASSUMED in the world bible. The camera plan itself was not supplied, so I cite only the IDs and figures the review notes give from it: C5, SH02A/SH02B, the 1.7x cut-in, and the frame sizes.
- Blocker (Cinematographer): The camera plan was drawn on the previous script's times: SH03 to 7.5 s, SH04-SH05 from 7.5 to 11.0 s, and SH06 as 96 f from 11.0 s. The revised script moves B3 to 4.5-7.0 and B4a/B4b to 7.0-8.5/8.5-11.5, puts B5-B6 at 11.5-15.0, and adds B2's thumb pass, which moves NORA's rack mark from y 248 to y 215. SH02A was cleared against the old mark. → If the plan hasn't been retimed yet, re-issue it with the following. SH03 at 4.5-7.0 (60 f). SH04-SH05 at 7.0-11.5 (108 f) as one take, joined if needed at the settle (nominal 8.5 s), with the buzz cued off the settle. SH06 at C5 as one locked 84 f hold from 11.5 s, cut in 1.7x from SH05. SH02A (18 f, 25°) and SH02B (54 f, 55°, seat on f15) keep their lengths. Re-check SH02A with NORA at y 215 (chest at y 196-234, x ≤195), and hold a tighter 25 cm variant for the thumb test. Hold a 30 cm SH05 variant on a bearing between 31° and 55° in case blocker 2 fails.
- Blocker (Production Designer (props/SFX)): The sound-off read of the B4b turn rests on the buzz rippling the coffee through the phone-to-mug contact. This is untested. Her fingertips on the mug and her heel on the oak may damp the vibration, and even real rings may be too small to read: in the 42 cm frame the coffee is about 1.2 cm wide on a 6-inch phone. → Run a pre-shoot test at SH05's frame (about 42 cm on 55°) at 100% speed. Set-up: the hand double's R in the B4b position (heel at about 225.7/295; pads over 225-315° below the rim, palm clear, thumb along the index); fresh hot coffee at the tide line with thin steam; the real phone in the PROP-02 case touching the mug on the PROP-07 oak; one buzz of about 0.8 s, fired on cue within 2 f. Pass: judged on a 6-inch phone at 100%, with the 7:05 card laid in, TILE-01's reflected edge is seen to bend in the coffee during the rattle. If it bends but doesn't read, re-test at about 30 cm on a bearing between 31° and 55°. If that passes, the settle join becomes plan of record, with SH05 reframed (ruling 4 reopened). If no rings form at any size, fit a concealed coin vibration motor inside the case's back plate, fire it on the buzz cue, and re-run both steps with the hand in place. If everything fails, SH05 is held as written and the turn rests on the still hand and the 7:05 card, with no composited rings. Tell the Copywriter and me before the shoot.
- Blocker (Production Designer (props)): The B3 pass mark (the whole bed darkened and the centre visibly risen, by at least about 5 mm, by 6.7 s) is a hypothesis: about 6-7 g of water lands on 18-20 g of grounds in a 1.6 s pour. PROP-05 ST4's 'domed about 1 cm' becomes the target, not the pass. → Run a pre-shoot bloom test on the real unit, at SH03's frame on 55°, at 100% speed. Coffee: roasted 5-14 days before the shoot and ground on the day, medium-fine, 18-20 g, heaped as in PROP-05 ST3. Water just off the boil, from PROP-06. Pour rate: about 4 g/s through the 5 mm spout, as two circuits in 1.6 s. Pass: the pass mark, read on a 6-inch phone at 100%. If it fails, B3 is staged as written, and the smallest fix goes to the Copywriter: change B3's picture from 'swell, bubble and dome up against the paper' to 'darken and start to rise', with no timing change.
- Blocker (Production Designer (props)): B4a needs a drop hanging at the exit hole when the take rolls, but PROP-01 ST4 says props drain the cone first 'so nothing else drips'. How a drop is there at 7.0 s is a hypothesis. → Amend PROP-01 ST4 so that props let the cone drain until drips slow to about one a second, and roll so the lift comes about 0.5-0.8 s after the last drip. Test at the prelight on the real unit. Pass: a drop falls into the coffee within 7.17-7.42 s in at least one take in three, and no second drop falls during the 0.42 s carry. If it fails, the smallest fix is the script's own B4a: the drop falls during the 3 cm lift, the hold goes, and its 4 f go to the release. The real detail stays: she doesn't carry the cone until the drop has fallen.
- Blocker (Copywriter): The script runs 'Nobody gets you before the pour.' across both B2 angles, so it comes in at 1.5 s on the same cut as the SH02A thumb pass (1.50-1.75 s), which sets up 'Hand-glazed stoneware.' A card coming up pulls the eye. This is pending the SH02A phone test. → Staged as written. If the phone test shows the card pulling the eye off the thumb, the smallest fix is for the card to come in on the SH02B cut at 2.25 s and hold to 4.5 s (2.25 s on screen). The Copywriter approves or refuses before the edit.
- Blocker (Producer / client services, then Casting and Production Designer): No real Solace unit or reference has been inspected. The world bible's glaze, colour and dimensions, including the 11.5 cm rim, are ASSUMED. SH02A, SH06 and SH08 depend on the real glaze pool (claim_demo_needs_clearance). Both of R's product grips cross the rim, so casting's 10.5 cm minimum applies. → Supply a production unit before any plate is shot, or two if the hand plates and the packshot shoot on the same day. Run the thumb-pass rehearsal, the bloom and drip tests, the packshot and the readability test on it. Confirm the rim diameter. If it is under 10.5 cm, Casting revises the hand spec, and I restage the SH02A/SH02B and SH04 grips. If the real foot shows no uneven pool, the PD picks a new swap-test detail from the real glaze, and the B2 thumb beat and SH06 are restaged on it.
- Blocker (Producer (release), with Casting (left-hand pour test)): Every hand plate is practical and uses a hand double: SH01, SH02A, SH02B, SH03 (L pour), SH04, SH05 and SH07. The double is a real person whose hands, with the mole and scar added, carry the fictional NORA across paid social and the DTC product page. The restage also needs the double to pour a steady, thin spiral left-handed. → Get a hand-talent release through the double's contract or agency booking. Scope: D01 9:16 paid social; the D02 stills, including use on the product page; term; territory. No generated use is needed, because every fallback is a pickup. The double must pass casting's left-hand pour test, and the pour is rehearsed at the prelight. Owner: Producer. Status: pending, not cleared. No hand plate is shot until the release is signed.

### Looks

- Season and temperature are ASSUMED: early November 2026, about 6 °C outside and 19 °C inside, in the world bible's assumed USA 2026 setting. The brief gives no season.
- The hue check uses the world bible's PROP-01 hexes (body #E3D9C6, pool #B59A72, rim #C9A77E, raw foot #A88663). These are ASSUMED, because the client gave no product colour. If the real glaze differs, rerun the check.
- All L*, C* and h values are hand-computed from hex (sRGB, D65) and approximate. New in this version: the counter's tinted oak #533C2B (L* 27, C* 16, h 62°) replaces #7A5A3E; the phone case #77756F (L* 49) replaces #3E4447; and the left-hand scar #8A6650 (L* 46, C* 21, h 58°) is added.
- I dropped the built callus. Casting now makes it 'backstory, not a lock', and no longer gives a size, hex or placement for it. A built mark that nobody checks drifts between setups and generations, so it is not built. Dropping it also removes the one skin tone that sat 11° from the raw foot at C* 22 inside the dripper grip. The review note's wording named 'the mole and callus', so the Generation line now names the mole on the right hand and the new scar on the left. Everything else is as the note asked: acceptance against CHR-NORA-HAND@v1, and no hex codes in prompts.
- I have not seen CHR-NORA-HAND@v1. It must show the mole on the right hand, the scar on the left, and no callus. If it doesn't, generation should reissue it before any hand frame is accepted.
- Casting's skin anchor says the words go 'with the hex codes' into every prompt. I follow the generation plan's rule, as the review note cites it: the words go in prompts, and the hexes go only in the hand-double call and the acceptance checks. Casting's line should be aligned to match.
- The left-hand scar is new from casting's anchors. Its length, colour, position and flatness are casting's. Its 1.5 mm width, tapered ends, side-to-side orientation and paint-not-transfer method are my ASSUMED build choices, for casting to sign off at the fitting.
- The director's restage makes the pour left-handed, so the left hand is on the kettle in D02-1. The shot list isn't in my context, so I don't know which hand appears in D01-B1, B2, B4a and B4b. The mark locks are therefore written per hand, not per beat.
- D01-B3 now shows only the spout, so the check-and-reapply setups drop B3 (it was listed in the last version).
- The mole transfer runs to 10 units from one file. That count, the 48 h patch test and the 7-day no-manicure window are my working assumptions.
- Knuckle creases, veins and forearm hair are matched at casting, not built or painted. If a double doesn't match, casting recasts. Casting left the forearm hair to the stylist, and it stays as it is.
- Hair colour, curl (2B), length and part are ASSUMED and never in frame. They are specified only so no strand enters a frame and the knot can be rebuilt.
- I kept the brown-black tee #2C2826 rather than the named alternative, #2B2C30. It is never in frame and it clears on C* 2. Swap it if any frame ever shows the neckline.
- Finished sizes (sleeve 52, body 58, chest 96, cuff 20 cm) and the felted weight (about 330 g/m²) are ASSUMED from the 167 cm casting spec. The fitting resets them on the hand double, and duplicates then match the hero within ±0.5 cm.
- One look only, because the script has no time jump. The cuff riding from 7 cm to 9 cm in a reach or pour is a fit tolerance, not a state change.
- I kept the ink blue. The darker oak (#533C2B) cuts the gap between sleeve and counter from 24 L* to 10 L*, but the 143° hue gap still keeps the two blocks apart. The sleeve still sits 18 L* below the skin, as casting's value rule asks. It stays outside the set palette on purpose, inside the world bible's 8% out-of-palette share.
- Pills are rubbed on an untinted offcut rather than on the new tinted PROP-07 finish, so no tint can transfer to the wool.
- The work coat and boots by the door are backstory only, and nothing is added to the set.
- No taste note is broken.
- No reference image was inspected. The cast is fictional, and no real person's likeness or name is used.

### Sound

- Timing: 24 fps, which matches the camera plan's numbering. Frames count from the start of D01 and equal round(seconds x 24). The current script beats are B1 f0-f36, B2 f36-f108, B3 f108-f168, B4a f168-f204, B4b f204-f276, B5 f276-f312 and B6 f312-f360. Every hit is picture-led, ±2 frames.
- The camera plan's action frames come via the review notes and outrank this plan: seat click f67; stream break on the selected SH03 take's break frame (planned f158-f164); last drip f189; set-down f203; B4b pulse f221-f240 (SH05 5f-24f). The client's f238 is inside ±2. Kept from the last version because the notes left them alone: buzz f2-f19, pad knock f30, rack knock f45-f48, kettle tip f113, stream lands f115, fizz from f127. The rest are my targets, for the editor to check against the camera plan's action lines: the lift-off tick, moved to f168-f170 with the new 7.0 s B3/B4a boundary; the ring-outs at f72 and f194; and the kettle ticks.
- FLAG for the film director and editor: the script puts the filter and grounds in during the B2/B3 cut, and B2 ends on the empty dripper. The earlier notes put a filter action in SH02B at f99-f105. Following the client's conditional note, no crinkle plays in the cut, and an alternate is recorded and placed at f99-f105 in case the edit keeps the SH02B action.
- FLAG for the editor and copywriter: the script brings the 7:05 card up at 9.5 s (f228) 'as the rings flatten' and calls the buzz 'about 1.0s'. On the camera plan the rattle runs f221-f240 (0.79 s), so a card at f228 lands mid-buzz. The sound follows the camera plan. The card is silent, so the sound doesn't change whichever card time is chosen, but the card-in probably wants to sit at or after f240.
- Card-in clearance for 007 is checked against the current script's f276 and f312, and also against the earlier review's f264 and f300, in case the storyboard still carries them.
- Buzz count: a single pulse, per the script's 'One phone buzz' and the route's 'buzzing once'. One pulse of at least 20 frames is recorded. B1 cuts it at the hand (f19); B4b plays it whole (f221-f240). One double-pulse take is recorded as a safety and not cut in. If it is ever used, 002 and 006 swap together.
- Phone physics follow PROP-02 as built, per the world bible as quoted in the notes: four corner pads 1.5 mm proud of the glass, bumper open along all four edges, flat case back with no raised camera module. Face-up it sits flush on the oak and gives a dense, even buzz. Face-down it rides on the pads and the glass never touches: the pads knock and chatter on the oak, and a corner pad ticks against the mug. The session records on a case built to that spec. Pad material isn't stated, so none is assumed.
- B4b hand: fingertips rest lightly on the mug's side with the palm clear, per the script. They damp the mug less than a full wrap would, so the pad ticks keep a brief, damped ceramic edge. The coffee rings are silent.
- Every D01 shot is a practical plate (generation_plan, via the notes). Production sound is a guide track only, and every cue is rebuilt from the foley session. No generated audio is planned.
- The kettle's off-screen position (burner at 214/100, hard right and slightly behind camera on the 55° axis, about 1.4 m from C4/C5) comes from the world bible, camera plan and treatment as quoted in the notes.
- Rights scope for the sounds recorded for this production (001, 002-007): a full assignment from the foley artist and the recordist covering all media, worldwide, in perpetuity, including use beyond D01. The 001 location release comes from the flat's occupant. All pending, owner producer.
- The scope for the 001 library fallback is an assumption to confirm: paid and organic social plus the Solace DTC site, one territory named by the producer, 12 months from first use.
- D02 is two stills with no audio, so it has no sound cues. The only deliverable id used in cues is D01.
- Invented world details, for production design to confirm against the world bible: the fridge hum and its position, the street traffic through a closed window, the coated-wire dish rack, and the kettle's metal body (D02-1's scorch band suggests metal).
- The coffee directions for capturing the bloom fizz are recording directions, not a product claim. 'Dense stoneware' is a sound direction only. The verified facts appear only in the silent cards.
- No taste notes are broken in sound. Followed: 'Sound design before music' (no music); 'Restraint over explanation' (no VO, silent cards, no foley on the thumb, the carry, the grab or the hold); 'Dry over sentimental' (no sighs or stings); 'The product is a tool, then a hero' (heard in use in B2-B4a, silent in the B5-B6 hold).

### Shot plan

- D01 runs at 24 fps because the spec gives none (logged): 36+24+48+60+36+72+84 = 360 frames = 15.0 s. The beat boundaries fall at B1 36f, B2 108f, B3 168f, B4a 204f, B4b 276f and B5-B6 360f. The D02 stills are logged at 24 fps, 0 frames.
- Lenses are full-frame equivalent: 9:16 on 20.25 x 36 mm, 4:5 on 24 x 30 mm. Frame percentages are pinhole estimates from the world-bible marks, ±3% (±5% for SH07); confirm them on the monitor. Depth of field uses a 0.03 mm circle of confusion.
- No platform template was given, so I assume a safe band at 15-65% of height and an icon rail over the right 12% of width, from 40% to 90% of height.
- Key: a unit behind WIN-01's diffusion frame, imitating an east window at 7:00 in early November. It runs at 4400K against a 5400K WB in D01 and D02-1, and SH08 is balanced neutral on purpose. ASSUMED; meter on the day.
- Hand speeds assume bell-shaped moves, with the peak about 1.6x the average, held to the treatment's 0.6 m/s.
- The '7:00' digit height is ASSUMED at 2.4 cm. The world bible's lock-screen spec (LOC-01) fixes the digits, colours and banner but gives no size.
- Coffee mirror, by the world-bible formula: from C4 the coffee centre mirrors wall D at about y 318, 119.6 cm; from C4' at about y 327, 119.5 cm. Both are geometric estimates, confirmed in the ring test.
- NORA is 167 cm (eye 156, shoulder 137). N2 and the rack mark come from the treatment. Product size and glaze come from the world bible and are ASSUMED; no reference was inspected.
- Deliberate departures: (1) SH02A runs 24f, not ruling 6's 18f. This is proposed and pending the director (blocker 6). With it the seat click lands at absolute f74 (3.08 s), 5 frames later than the treatment's f69 (SH02B f15 after an 18f SH02A), because SH02B now opens 12 cm out, as the treatment had it. (2) SH04's arc takes 14f, not 10f, because 21 cm in 10f would peak near 0.8 m/s. (3) SH06 and SH08 are at f/16, so the whole dripper stays sharp with focus on the front arc. (4) SH07 is on 55°, not ~70°. (5) Card zones are set on screen, not in the wall-D card area (blocker 3). (6) Fill for SH04-SH06 and SH08 is a CEIL-01 bounce with a lens flag. In SH06 and SH08 it becomes one defined patch that makes the pool band the brightest detail, and the key is netted off the frame-left flank. (7) C1 moved from 172/264, 175 cm (55°/50°, digits leaning 42.5°) to 180/282, 187 cm (65°/60°, about 27°), so the earlier 1.09 m / 54.9° / 49.5° figures no longer apply. (8) SH05's fallback is rebuilt at C4', so the dripper stays whole in frame and the coffee still mirrors TILE-01.
- Closed per the director's rulings: the rack is out of SH01 (1), the phone's end is kept in SH03 (3), the wrist crosses the port in SH05 (5), and B2 is split with SH04-SH05 as one take (4). The PD's grommet puts OUT-01 below the top, out of every frame and reflection. The filter stack is gone from B2 (script).
- Taste notes followed: hands before faces (no face anywhere); no slow motion (100% speed); no window and no gazing; the product as a tool before it is the hero; the end frame on the product, not a logo card. One break, which is the script's call: S2's scorch band is out of D01 so the bloom stays large; it reads in SH07.
- Acceptance checks and pass marks belong to the generation supervisor. The SH03 pass mark is dropped. The tests in the blockers only settle framing, exposure and lens choices.
- Blocker (producer): The hand-double release is pending. The double's hands appear in the SH01, SH02A, SH02B, SH03 (pouring), SH04 and SH05 plates and in the SH07 still, and may be used as a generator reference. → Secure a release from the double's agent covering the D01 and D02 plates and reference use before any plate is shot or fed to generation.
- Blocker (producer): Two framing questions need the built set. (a) SH05's ring read: at 65mm and 1.39 m the coffee is about 1.2 × 0.7 cm on a 6-inch screen, and the turn depends on the rings visibly bending TILE-01's reflected edge. (b) SH07's two locks: the scar joint at least 6% inside frame, and the frame top at least 5% below WIN-01's sill. → Schedule both tests before the shoot day. Ring test: in SH05's frame, with the unit's top cut in place, judged on a 6-inch phone at 100% with the card on. If it passes, shoot SH04-SH05 as one take. If it fails, shoot SH05 from C4' (100mm, 1.37 m) as its own take, cut at 8.5 s. SH07 frame test at C6: if both locks can't hold, keep the sill out and refer the scar to the director.
- Blocker (director): The treatment places the cards in the wall-D card area (125 cm and up). In SH02B that area falls at 0-11% h, above the safe band, and it is above the frame in SH02A and SH04-SH06. The clear screen zone in B2 (16-26% h) lies over TILE-01's course and, after SH02A's tilt, over its bullnose end. The SH05 fallback's card sits on the wall-D paint at 15-21% h. → Approve the screen zones: 16-26% h in B2, bare oak in B4b and B5-B6, and the wall-D paint at 15-21% h in the SH05 fallback. The storyboard checks card legibility over the tile band and the bullnose.
- Blocker (client): Not yet verified: the read of the pool band in SH06 and SH08 (the 12 mm arc is estimated at about 42 px) and its level against the netted flank, the mirror contents with no rig, the real rim diameter (casting needs 10.5 cm or more), and f/16 sharpness on the real unit. → Supply the real unit for a camera test at the SH06 and SH08 frames with the CEIL-01 patch rigged, graded on a 6-inch phone at 100% with the cards on. Keep 1.25 m if it passes and move to 1.15 m if not. If both fail, refer to the director and copywriter.
- Blocker (director): Route departure: R1 asks for a 'handheld-but-steady' camera, and every shot here is locked. The '7:00' composite, the product's own output at 100mm, the S3 take and the S4 hold all need stillness. → Approve the locked grammar with Lucas, or approve handheld for S1 only, at the cost of a tracked '7:00' composite.
- Blocker (director): Ruling 6 sets SH02A at 18f. At the 0.6 m/s cap, the entry and hook take about 6f and the thumb sweep about 8f, which leaves 4f for a 14 cm lift. This plan runs SH02A at 24f and SH02B at 48f; no upstream page supports that change yet. → Rule on 24f. If 18f stands: 0-6f entry and hook, 6-14f the sweep, 14-18f the lift starts (about 5 cm) and the cut falls mid-lift. SH02B then runs 54f, opens as planned, and the click lands at absolute f68. B2 stays 72f either way.
- Blocker (production_designer): SH02A's 4° steeper tilt puts its bottom-right corner about 7 cm into the dry sink (y≈178). FAUCET-01 stays far outside frame-right, but the world bible asks for it to be built when any frame shows y<185. → Confirm that the rule is met with the faucet out of frame, or build FAUCET-01 for SH02A.

### Storyboard

- Each card runs the whole script beat unless the script gives a time. B2 runs from SH02A f0 through SH02B f47; B4b from SH05 f24 (the script's 9.5 s) to f71; B5 is SH06 f0-f35 and B6 is SH06 f36-f83; the 'Solace' sign-off runs f0-f83.
- Panel frames are in-shot frames at 24 fps, converted from the sound plan's absolute frames using the current shot starts: SH01 f0, SH02A f36, SH02B f60, SH03 f108, SH04 f168, SH05 f204, SH06 f276.
- Sound-plan ranges are read start-inclusive and end-exclusive, the plan's own convention: the B1 pulse 'f2-f19' stops on f19, where its tone-alone window starts. A one-frame hit sits on its own frame, with room tone alone from the next. This replaces last round's inclusive reading.
- The front is boarded at the bible's 235° in every state, square to the bearing-55° lenses and 30° off in SH02A.
- NORA's L is placed only where the camera plan places it: flat on the oak at y≈320 in SH02B, and on the kettle handle in SH03 and SH07. Elsewhere it is 'off frame, not placed'. R hangs at her side in SH03 and SH07, per the film director's note.
- NORA at the rack in SH02A comes from the camera plan's torso note ('27 cm left of the line and 31 cm nearer'). Her walk there falls in the SH01/SH02A cut, per the script's B1 decision, and her walk back falls in the SH02A/SH02B cut.
- The carry grip in SH02A-SH02B (index and middle fingers over the front rim, thumb on the outer rim band after crossing the pool) is read from SH02A's action and SH02B's 'grips it from the lens side'.
- The SH04-SH05 grip wording merges the three review notes with the bible's PROP-03 ST4: 'R finger pads along the mug's aisle side over 225-315°, below the rim, palm clear, heel on the oak at 225.7/295'.
- The camera plan's 'ring' is read as the set-down mark 232.7/280, not a visible stain, because the bible keeps the work zone clean (see RING NAME).
- The ink-blue cuff is boarded toward the frame-left edge in SH04-SH05, from the B4a look lock and the roughly 12 cm of forearm in frame against a sleeve that ends 7-9 cm above the wrist. The camera plan doesn't place it.
- PROP-08 in SH04-SH05: the plug at the phone's port end is in frame, where the wrist crosses it (ruling 5). The camera plan lists the rest of the route without placing it.
- The phone's screen goes dark as it lands face-down in SH01 (PROP-02 ST2), so SH05's edge glow is the first.
- The picture window for SH02A's knock is read as 14f (the lift starts) to about 22f (the foot clears the wires), because the camera plan's 'faint knock off the tray' fixes no frame.
- The D02 stills carry no audio, and the sound plan covers D01 only, so SH07 and SH08 have no cues.
- Paid is boarded as the primary cut. The organic alternates are noted word for word beside it.
- The kettle's burner is boarded at the bible's 212/97. The sound plan pans from 214/100, a difference of under 4 cm that changes nothing audible.
- '7:00' and '7:05' frame the ritual. They are not a brew-time or performance claim (a hypothesis for the producer to confirm).
- tracked_elements logs a panel only where the element is in frame or heard. The declared exceptions are NORA in SH03 (her pour is the in-frame action, with L out frame-left) and PROP-06 in SH06 (heard, SND-D01-007). PROP-07 is logged only where the set-down mark is in frame (SH04-SH08); elsewhere its ST1 is written into the panel states.
- Every generation_risk line stays 'high', because each shot fails in a named way if generated. The PATH at the head of each line tells the generation supervisor whether a generator touches the shot at all.
- No reference was inspected, because no reference_reader is bound. Positions and states come only from the camera plan, the bibles and the script given here.
- Blocker (producer): PROP-01's whole appearance (shape, size, colour, glaze, foot pool, raw foot) is still ASSUMED in the world bible. The pool now carries the SH02A thumb pass, B5/B6, D02-2 and the SH06/SH08 lighting, and the SH02B seat depends on the assumed raw band. → Get the production sample and the brand's confirmation of its appearance, and supply a real-sample reference before any product frame is generated, dressed or shot. If the real unit differs, the copywriter re-picks the mark, the cinematographer relights SH06 and SH08, and the SH02B seat is re-planned.
- Blocker (producer (for Lucas, with the cinematographer)): Route camera: every shot is locked off (camera plan blocker 5), against route R1's 'handheld-but-steady camera'. The departure is not approved. → Lucas approves the locked grammar before the shoot, or the camera plan is redone handheld-but-steady and the board with it.
- Blocker (producer (for Lucas, with the cinematographer)): Route light: SH08 (D02-2) is balanced neutral (a 5000K key on a 5000K WB), against route R1's 'warm low window light', and it pairs with a warm SH07. The departure is not approved. → Lucas approves the neutral DTC still, or the cinematographer also shoots SH08 warm and Lucas chooses on the plates.
- Blocker (producer): The hand double's release is still pending. She is a real person, and SH01, SH02A, SH02B, SH04, SH05 and SH07 rely on her hands. → Get a signed release covering paid social, the DTC site and the stills before any hand plate is shot or used as a generation reference.

### Generation plan

- No adapter is bound. The conservative profile is the yardstick: image-to-video from one image, 5 s clips, 24 fps, 720p, no seed or negative prompt; images at 1024 px from one reference. No job needs it, because all nine panels carry generation_risk high and name a practical or composite path.
- Cost: the worst case is 0 generation units. No unit price is needed or sourced, so no ceiling is asked of Lucas. The director chose a pickup for SH02A, so SH02A-FB-IMG and SH02A-FB-VID are deleted, along with their 720p and price-ceiling blockers and both missing capabilities. A generated fallback, if ever wanted, would be re-planned and priced before anything runs.
- All reference IDs are placeholders for files still to be shot, made or supplied: REF-PROP01-SAMPLE@v1 (V1-V8), CHR-NORA-HAND@v1, LOOK-NORA-L01@v1, REF-PROP02-GLOW@v1, PASS-SH05-GLOW@v1, ART-LOCK-0700@v1, PLT-SH01@v1 to PLT-SH06@v1, STL-SH07@v1 and STL-SH08@v1. None has been viewed, and no reference_reader is bound.
- Timings are in-shot seconds at 24 fps, taken from the storyboard's action frames. SH01: 14f = 0.58 s, 19f = 0.8 s, 29f = 1.2 s, 36f = 1.5 s. SH02A: 6f = 0.25 s, 14f = 0.58 s, 21f = 0.88 s, 22f = 0.92 s, 24f = 1.0 s. SH02B: 10f = 0.42 s, 14f = 0.58 s, 19f = 0.79 s, 35f = 1.46 s, 48f = 2.0 s. SH03: 5f = 0.21 s, 10f = 0.42 s, 48f = 2.0 s, 53f = 2.21 s, 60f = 2.5 s. SH04: 4f = 0.17 s, 6f = 0.25 s, 10f = 0.42 s, 20f = 0.83 s, 25f = 1.04 s, 33f = 1.38 s, 36f = 1.5 s. SH05: 5f = 0.21 s, 24f = 1.0 s, 30f = 1.25 s, 72f = 3.0 s. SH06: 84f = 3.5 s. The sound plan's differing cue frames (SYNC) are the sound designer's to move.
- '7:00' size, corrected: SH01's frame is 34 cm wide (65mm at 1.1 m on a 20.25 mm gate), not 49 cm. 1080 ÷ 34 ≈ 32 px per cm. At 2.4 cm cap height that is about 76 px flat and about 65 px on screen after roughly 0.85 foreshortening (2.4 × 1080/34 × 0.85). The camera plan now also assumes 2.4 cm and gives 65-75 px, so 2.4 cm is the art spec of record in ART-LOCK-0700. The measured pass mark stays at 45 px or more.
- The key comes from frame-left in every setup. Every lens sits on the aisle side with wall C at frame-left, and there is no reverse.
- Identity lives in CHR-NORA-HAND@v1 and the acceptance checks: the mole on the right hand, the scar on the left index finger. The callus is dropped from the checks, because casting makes it backstory, not a lock. Prompts carry no hex codes. The style bible's prompt words for the hand apply only to generated frames, and none are planned.
- PROP-01's appearance stays ASSUMED until the sample is signed off (CLAIM). Prompts say 'the dripper', 'its marked front, as in REF-PROP01-SAMPLE@v1' and 'its mark', with no colour or finish words for the product. SH06 and SH08 carry the client's fallback check for a unit with no pool.
- RING NAME: 232.7/280 is called the set-down mark on clean oak throughout, and any ring, stain or drip fails a check.
- Where the newer storyboard differs from last round's review notes, the storyboard wins. SH02B clicks at 14f (0.58 s), not 0.3 s. SH07 now locks the left-index scar in frame, so it gets a scar mirror check. SH02A is planned at the boarded 24f, pending the director (blocker). The grip wording the reviewers asked for is applied word for word in SH04-PLT and SH05-PLT.
- Frame contents follow the world bible: no filter or filter stack on the counter (dropped from SH01), and the rack holds a plate and a tumbler only, with no fork. SH02B has no filter action, because the filter goes in during the SH02B/SH03 cut. The phone case is stone-grey.
- Prompts write each exclusion as the wanted state ('only the spout enters', 'only those fingers show of her', 'bare oak on every side of the foot'). The acceptance checks name what fails.
- Triage scores only the craft brief's ten factors. The phone, mug and kettle are props, so handling them is not product contact.
- These jobs break no taste note. SH03 plays at 100% speed. Hands are shown and no face. Nobody looks out of the window. The rack and cable are in frame. The product is used before its hero shots, is in D01's last frame and stands alone in the DTC still. SH03's missing scorch band is the script's declared break, carried by SH07.
- Picture carries no speech. Every video prompt ends on ambient room tone only, and the sound designer owns all audio.
